# Tasks — core-memory-kit v0.1.0

**Status**: Draft · **Author**: Claude (Opus 4.7) + the maintainer · **Date started**: 2026-05-23

This is the **actionable implementation plan**. A numbered, hierarchical checklist of concrete coding steps that build incrementally from an empty repo to a working v0.1.0 release. The bridge between [`requirements.md`](requirements.md) (WHAT v0.1.0 must do), [`design.md`](design.md) (HOW v0.1.0 is built), and [`glossary.md`](glossary.md) (canonical definitions for every domain term).

> **This file holds LIVE work; finished entries live in [`tasks-archive.md`](tasks-archive.md).** On 2026-07-28 (Task 249) the full text of **211** finished parent tasks — the shipped-note retrospectives that had grown this file to 918 KB — moved to the archive **byte-identical**, each leaving a one-line pointer behind:
>
> `- [x] NNN. _<verb> <date>, PR #N_ — **<short title>** → [archive](tasks-archive.md)`
>
> **Every task id that ever existed is still in THIS file** (as a pointer or in full), so the id sequence is unbroken and a `Task N` citation from anywhere still resolves. `—` in a pointer means the entry genuinely carries no date/PR — it is not a lookup failure. Ids covered: **1–257**, minus the ones still open or restored below.
>
> **The going-forward rule — two halves, both binding:**
>
> 1. **Completed entries move at ARCHIVE time, not at completion time.** A task that ships keeps its full entry here — that is where the next session reads what just landed. A later, deliberate archiving pass relocates it. Never archive an entry in the same change that closes it.
> 2. **A parent is archive-eligible only when the PARENT *and every one of its sub-boxes* is closed or spun off.** A `[x]` parent carrying live `- [ ]` sub-tasks is still live work, and freezing it would bury trigger-bearing scope inside a record nobody is allowed to edit. This criterion was written after the first pass archived 8 such parents (45, 46, 50, 51, 68, 76, 78, 116) and they were restored — check the sub-boxes, not just the parent.
>
> When you do run a pass: append the moved entries to the END of the archive under a dated `## Relocated YYYY-MM-DD` heading, leave the pointer shape above behind, and **do not reword a moved entry** — relocation only.

The fix (the user's call): emit **lint-clean markdown by construction** — NOT relax the rules. Full read+write audit done; the SAFE path is narrow (see Task 164 sub-tasks + D-194-adjacent decision records). **Code/workflows/YAML/JSON all pass clean** (actionlint et al. find nothing — only markdown is at issue).

## How to read this file

Tasks follow Kiro convention:

- **`- [ ] N.` parent tasks** group related work (one focused area, ~1–3 PR-days each).
- **`- [ ] N.M` sub-tasks** are concrete, actionable implementation steps. Max two levels of nesting.
- **`- [ ]* N.last` (asterisk)** marks the test sub-task. Optional for MVP-only paths; required otherwise. _All tests are run by the agent (Claude), not by a human._
- **`_Requirements: FR-X; design §Y_`** on the test sub-task traces back to the spec.
- Plain bullets under any task line are implementation notes / context.
- **`- [ ] N. Checkpoint — ...`** standalone numbered items between layers gate progress. Agent runs the full test suite and confirms zero failures before moving on.
- **`- [x] N. … → [archive](tasks-archive.md)`** is a **pointer**, not an entry: the task is finished and its full text (sub-tasks, shipped notes, retrospective) lives in [`tasks-archive.md`](tasks-archive.md). Follow the link before assuming anything about what it did. Adding to the archive is a deliberate pass, never part of closing a task — see the header block for the two-part rule (archive-time-not-completion-time; parent **and** all sub-boxes closed).

Domain terms used below (Tier, Citation ID, Frozen snapshot, Poison_Guard, etc.) are defined in [`glossary.md`](glossary.md).

## Engineering discipline

1. **Test-driven**: Write the test first, let the agent implement, verify, repeat. Small cycles, high confidence.
2. **Boundary testing** (per Ousterhout, _A Philosophy of Software Design_): Test the public interface of each module, not its internal helpers. Test the contract that survives refactors.
3. **Deep modules with simple interfaces**: When implementing, wrap related code into broad modules that expose narrow surfaces. Don't fragment cohesive logic into many shallow helpers.
4. **Incremental build**: Each task produces working, integrated code. No orphaned code. After any sub-task, you can run what exists and verify it works.
5. **Code review at strategic boundaries** (not per-PR): tests are catching unit-level bugs; reviews target what tests miss. Three layer-wide reviews at Checkpoints 11, 27, 42 via the `code-review-excellence` skill. Plus individual PR reviews on Tasks 23 (auto-extract), 24 (memory-write + Poison_Guard), 31 (MCP server) — high-risk surface. See those entries for specifics. For all other tasks, just build them normally — no per-PR review step.

  - **Posture pivot (decision-trail) — manual gate → optimistic auto-promote.** The original design below (45.1 Design A/B + 45.2 manual `cmk persona accept/reject` + 45.3 `--auto` opt-in **default-off**) is a MANUAL-confirmation shape. It is **superseded for v0.2** by the user's 2026-05-30 automatic-memory decision (*"i dont want to do anything, i want it to be automatic"*; chose **Optimistic**). New default posture (see 45.6 below): auto-promote cross-project doctrine to the user tier without a manual accept step; route only LOW-confidence candidates to the auto-drained review queue; auto-supersede on contradiction (reuse Task 25 conflict detection). The original 45.1-45.5 manual design is **preserved** as the decision-trail and as the fallback if optimistic auto-promote proves too aggressive in real use.
  - Uses shared modules from `packages/cli/src/{tier-paths,audit-log,frontmatter,result-shapes}.mjs` + scratchpad.mjs (Task 12) + provenance.mjs (Task 13). Consumes auto-extract output from Task 23. See design.md §16.16 for full motivation + design.
  - **Pre-implementation: read [`docs/research/2026-05-24-tencentdb-agent-memory.md`](../docs/research/2026-05-24-tencentdb-agent-memory.md) (auto-persona-every-N-facts source pattern) + [`docs/research/2026-05-25-claude-remember-code-dive.md`](../docs/research/2026-05-25-claude-remember-code-dive.md) (identity-candidates inline-surfacing pattern — architectural simplification, see 45.1 below).** License: TencentDB is MIT (port freely with attribution); claude-remember is Community License (absorb idea, write our own).
- [ ] 45.1 Implement persona-candidate surfacing — **two design choices**
  - **Design A (separate pipeline stage)**: `cmk persona generate` subcommand reads `<userDir>/memory/` + cross-project persona-tagged facts, synthesizes via `CompressorBackend` (default `HaikuViaAnthropicApi` per §8.3), outputs candidate updates staged at `<userDir>/queues/persona-review.md` with full provenance. Triggered manually or every-N-facts from auto-extract (Task 23). Honors 120s Haiku cooldown.
  - **Design B (inline in consolidator)**: piggyback on Task 34's weekly consolidation pass. The consolidator already runs the CompressorBackend over recent.md content; extend its prompt with a "Step 3: identity candidates" instruction that surfaces persona-relevant moments at the end of recent.md as `## Identity Candidates\n- IDENTITY CANDIDATE: <description>` bullets. Zero additional API calls. `cmk persona generate` becomes a thin wrapper that reads these candidates from recent.md and stages them in `queues/persona-review.md`. **Source:** claude-remember's actual implementation (see code-dive note §"Transition 3" + the Step 3 prompt fragment); architectural simplification worth pre-implementation decision.
  - **Decision deferred to implementation time** (after Task 34 ships and we see whether the consolidator's prompt budget has headroom for the extra instruction). Recommendation: try Design B first; fall back to Design A if the consolidator can't reliably surface candidates without hurting compression quality.
- [ ] 45.2 Implement `cmk persona accept <id>` / `cmk persona reject <id>` subcommands
  - `accept`: matched bullet promoted into target scratchpad via `appendScratchpadBullet` (Task 12) with `trust: high` (manual user-attestation)
  - `reject`: candidate removed from `queues/persona-review.md`; rejection recorded as an anti-signal fact in `<userDir>/memory/persona_rejections.md` (informs future syntheses — auto-extract sees the pattern and skips it)
  - Both write audit-log entries via `appendAuditEntry` per design §6.1
- [ ] 45.3 Implement auto-apply mode behind `--auto` flag and `settings.json` opt-in
  - `cmk persona generate --auto` writes directly to target scratchpad via `appendScratchpadBullet` with `trust: medium` (system-derived, not user-attested)
  - Per-scratchpad opt-in: `settings.json` key `persona.auto_apply: ["USER.md", "HABITS.md"]` lists which scratchpads accept auto-apply (default: empty array — explicit opt-in only)
  - One audit-log entry per change, including the source-fact ID(s) the synthesis was derived from
- [ ] 45.4 Conflict-with-hand-curated handling
  - When a proposed update conflicts with an existing `trust: high` entry (substring similarity >0.85 + different text), stage in `<userDir>/queues/persona-conflict.md` rather than silent overwrite
  - Consistent with design §6.2 conflict queue + §6.8 (memory-write conflict resolution; user resolves via `cmk queue conflicts`)
  - Never silently demote a `trust: high` hand-curated entry to make room for a system-generated proposal
- [ ]* 45.5 Write unit tests
  - Test `cmk persona generate` with mocked Haiku: produces candidate bullets in `persona-review.md` with provenance
  - Test `accept` promotes candidate to scratchpad at `trust: high`; audit log records the promotion
  - Test `reject` removes candidate from queue + writes rejection fact; audit log records
  - Test `--auto` mode writes directly at `trust: medium`; opt-in gating via settings.json enforced
  - Test conflict-with-hand-curated: high-trust entry NOT overwritten; candidate staged in `persona-conflict.md`
  - Test 120s Haiku cooldown honored
  - _Requirements: FR-3, FR-10, FR-29; design §16.16 + §6.2 + §8.3_

The queue stays a safety net for the uncertain, never a required step for the obvious.
  - **Auto-supersede on contradiction**: when a promoted persona fact contradicts an existing one, reuse Task 25 conflict detection to atomically supersede (close old, write new) rather than silent coexist — closes self-test finding #3 Gap B (no cross-store dedup).
  - **Never silently demote `trust: high` hand-curated entries** (45.4's invariant still holds): a contradiction against a high-trust hand-curated fact stages in `persona-conflict.md`, it does not auto-overwrite.
  - **Manual override still works**: `cmk persona accept/reject` (45.2) and `cmk lessons promote` remain available for users who want explicit control; they're just no longer REQUIRED for the common case.

## v0.2 — Automatic memory + "Claude stays consistent" (the kit's core vision)

> Formalized 2026-05-30 from [`docs/journey/v0.1.1-self-test-findings.md`](../docs/journey/v0.1.1-self-test-findings.md) §"COMMITTED ROADMAP" after the Phase-1 self-test sweep landed (v0.1.2 shipped). The user: *"this was an integral part of the kit … if it's somewhere in the future I want to be there in that future, and now."* Dependency-ordered phases; **the friend handoff can happen after Phase 2 lands** (user tier finally populates), Phase 3 is the headline value.

**Phase 2 — Automatic memory (kill every manual step).** Make capture→promotion fully hands-off.
- **Task 45 (auto-persona, optimistic auto-promote — 45.6 above)** — the friend-handoff gate. Cross-project doctrine auto-lands in the user tier.
- **Auto-drain the review/conflict queues** — the daily-distill (Task 33) + weekly-curate (Task 34) passes drain `queues/review.md` + `queues/conflicts.md` automatically (optimistic auto-promote + auto-supersede). No manual `cmk queue *` step in the common case. ✅ _Shipped 2026-05-30, PR #86: `packages/cli/src/auto-drain.mjs` (`autoDrainQueues` — review→promote, conflict→keep-old) wired into both passes (daily drains P-tier; weekly drains P + U); the manual `cmk queue *` verbs still work. Glossary: [[Auto-drain]]; decision-log D-6._

**Phase 3 — The heart: "Claude remembers its own positions and stays consistent."** The FastAPI scenario (*"2 days ago you said X was a mistake; now you say the opposite"*). Three pieces (findings doc §"v0.2 headline capability"):
1. **AI-side position capture** — capture Claude's stated positions ("recommended X", "advised against Y") as timestamped, contradiction-checkable decision-facts (today only user prefs + project facts are captured; the assistant's own positions are not).
2. **Recall the dialogue** — index `context/transcripts/{date}.md` for search (today transcripts are captured to disk but NOT indexed and NOT injected) + inject a compact "recent decisions" digest at SessionStart. **Subsumes Task 51** (index session-rollup + transcript files for search).
3. **Contradiction-awareness/reconciliation** — prior position in context so Claude notices a reversal and reconciles (explain the change or self-correct). Reuses the optimistic auto-promote + auto-supersede engine from Phase 2.
- **Task 55 (behavioral pattern detection)** — extends into Phase 3; detect + promote recurring working-style patterns (depends on Task 45).

_Design home: design §16.16 (auto-persona), §16.18 (temporal/validity-windows — serves contradiction reconciliation), §16.52 (behavioral patterns). Each Phase-2/3 task gets full sub-tasks + tests when its implementation PR opens, per the per-task ship cycle._

  - [x] 50.O.1 **KG-guard delete-guardrail didn't fire — matcher fix (D-197, #224).** The `preToolUse` matcher used pipe-alternation; kiro-cli matchers are literal strings → `matcher: '*'`. A real-but-SECONDARY fix (the true cause was 50.O.2). _shipped 2026-06-24._
  - [x] 50.O.2 **THE root cause — agent config written to the WRONG location (D-198, #225).** The kit wrote `~/.aws/amazonq/cli-agents/q_cli_default.json`; kiro-cli loads from `~/.kiro/agents/<name>.json` + `~/.kiro/settings/cli.json` `chat.defaultAgent`. So OUR config was NEVER read → NO hooks fired. Rewrote to the correct location + default registration; dropped the invalid `managedBy` field (→ marker in `description`), the `includeMcpJson`/`useLegacyMcpJson` duplicate, and `file://` resource refs (resolve relative to the agent dir, not the project). LIVE-PROVEN: `kiro-cli agent list` shows `* cmk Global`, `agentSpawn` FIRES → inject + capture work. _shipped 2026-06-24._
  - [x] 50.O.3 **Explicit memory silently did nothing — the missing `tools` capability field (D-199, #226).** `cmk remember`/`cmk search` in chat reported `✅ Remembered` but wrote NOTHING — the agent config had no `tools` field, so a custom agent had ZERO tools enabled and could run no shell command (the model FABRICATED success). Four prior diagnoses blamed Kiro bugs (#5873/#5376/#7431/#4579) — all wrong. **Fix: `tools: ['*']`** + `includeMcpJson:false` (no MCP → no cmd.exe popup; uses the `cmk remember`/`cmk search` shell commands) + a `--project` flag (prompt tells the model to pass it, never `cd`). Cleaned the dead MCP machinery (−46 lines). LIVE-PROVEN (a real rich fact landed). _shipped 2026-06-24. CLAUDE.md binding lesson added: "check the agent's capability/enablement config FIRST when a tool call fabricates success."_

LIVE-PROVEN end-to-end: capture reads the real CLI transcript → auto-extract → the WEDGE promotes (a cross-project rule landed in `~/.core-memory-kit/HABITS.md`). Plus the kiro.dev primary-source deep-research (no SessionEnd on any surface; IDE has 10 triggers not 2). _shipped 2026-06-25. Net: kiro-cli CORE memory fully works + live-proven._

- [x] 51. _closed 2026-07-19, PR #308 (absorbed by Task 126 + 226/227; facts-scope promotion rejected — D-362)._ **→ TRIO VERDICT 2026-07-19 (D-362): CLOSE as ABSORBED — the remaining scope shipped as Task 126's transcript-chunk scope + 226/227's polish.** `recent.md`/`archive.md`/`today-*.md` ARE searchable today (`cmk search --scope transcripts`, the raw-tier index that deliberately excludes the volatile `now.md` — exactly this task's design caution), with dates (227) and neighborhood expansion (226). The residual ask — promoting rollups into the DEFAULT facts scope via `--include-sessions` — is REJECTED with reasons: it would duplicate hits (facts extracted from the same content), churn the observations index (this task's own caution), and blur the curated-vs-record scope split the memory-search skill teaches (transcripts = the last-resort rung BY DESIGN). The "is this even needed?" doubt below was right. Original scope preserved per decision-trail: **→ SWEEP VERDICT 2026-07-18 (D-353): trigger FIRED — v0.6.0 IS the recall-surface minor (226/227). Scheduled into the v0.6.0 recall batch.** Index session-rollup + transcript files for search (T-039) _— disposition made explicit 2026-07-10 (D-311; it had free-ridden on the long-shipped v0.3.0 bucket): recall lane, version-agnostic; trigger: rides the next recall-surface batch (149/176/178) or v0.5.3's search work, whichever first. (Transcript-chunk search itself shipped via Task 104; this task's remaining scope is the session-ROLLUP files.)_
  - Estimate: M · Depends: 28, 29 · **v0.2 candidate (LOW priority — see "is this even needed?" below)**
  - **What**: extend `cmk search` / `cmk reindex` to index the session-compression files (`sessions/today-*.md`, `recent.md`, `archive.md`) and optionally `transcripts/*.md`, not just durable memory (`MEMORY.md` + `memory/*.md`). Today `index-rebuild.mjs::listObservationSources` deliberately skips these (line: `Caller-skipped: today-{date}.md`).
  - **Why it surfaced**: 2026-05-29 live test — `cmk search "PostgreSQL"` returned nothing because those facts only lived in the session-rollup layer (`today.md`/`recent.md`), which isn't indexed. Full write-up: [`docs/journey/v0.1.0-live-test.md`](../docs/journey/v0.1.0-live-test.md) Finding 1. Maps to requirements.md US-9 ("search across … sessions, and transcripts").
  - **IS THIS EVEN NEEDED? (read before building)**: In real usage, durable facts stated in conversation are ALREADY searchable in v0.1.0 — auto-extract writes them to MEMORY.md (which IS indexed) on every Stop hook. The live-test gap was a test artifact (compression ran on that content, auto-extract did not). This task only adds value for content that lives SOLELY in session rollups and was never auto-extracted (e.g., narrative "what happened" summaries). Validate that gap is real with a user before building — it may be redundant with auto-extract coverage.
  - **Design caution**: session rollups churn (now→today→recent→archive rotate constantly); indexing them means high index turnover. The chokidar watcher (Task 29) would re-index on every compression pass. Consider indexing only `archive.md` (stable) + `recent.md`, not the volatile `today-*.md`/`now.md`. Or gate behind a `--include-sessions` flag (off by default).

- [ ] 51.3 `cmk search --include-sessions` flag (off by default to avoid index churn)
- [ ]* 51.4 Tests — seed recent.md/archive.md, reindex with includeSessions, assert session content is searchable; assert default (no flag) still excludes them

This adds the refinement: auto-extract actively **detects recurring behavioral patterns** ("always wants X format", "runs tests before commit", "prefers terse replies") and **promotes** them into `HABITS.md` / the persona, instead of leaving them as scattered one-off facts. A ranking/promotion step on the existing extract→trust→scratchpad pipeline — no new storage model.
  - **Scope boundary**: this is ONLY the clean refinement (detect + promote behavioral patterns as memory). The bigger ECC-style **procedural memory** (turn patterns into actionable reusable *skills/procedures*) is a separate, larger direction — explicitly OUT of scope, evidence-gated to a later call. Don't conflate.
  - **Sketch** (design §16.52): (1) recurrence signal in auto-extract (same canonicalized observation seen N times, or an "always/usually/prefer" cue); (2) promote to `HABITS.md` / user-tier persona with a `pattern_count`/confidence field (numeric refinement of the categorical trust level — the one idea worth borrowing from ECC); (3) surface in the frozen snapshot like other high-trust memory. Composes with Task 45 (shared promotion target), trust hierarchy (§4), review queue (medium-confidence → confirm).
  - **Design input — U-Mem contrastive failure-memory + the self-diagnosis constraint (2026-07-01, [U-Mem note](../docs/research/2026-07-01-umem-autonomous-memory.md), D-251) — THIRD independent source for the error→fix type.** U-Mem (arXiv:2602.22406, NUS) types failure-memory as **Global Procedural** ("the correct workflow/recipe for this class") vs **Local Corrective** ("in context X, don't assume Y, do Z") — adopt that split for the error→fix shape (Global ≈ the `Prevention`/lesson field, Local ≈ the `Root Cause`+`Solution` pair). **The load-bearing constraint (out-of-the-box, applies to ALL of Task 55's capture):** *"you cannot self-diagnose a failure without a correct reference."* U-Mem's evidence shows naïve self-reflection on a wrong trajectory HALLUCINATES the cause — the agent lacks the knowledge to identify its own error (their MemRL baseline extracted "trivial but correct" details that missed the real flaw). **Implication for our auto-extract:** reflecting on a session ALONE (which is what we do) is structurally weak for failure-lessons; the *correct reference* in a single-user kit is usually **the user's correction** ("no, do it this way"). So the highest-value capture signal for a behavioral/error pattern is the user-correction turn — a good-vs-bad contrast, not a solo reflection. Design 55's recurrence/cue detection to WEIGHT user-correction turns as the reference trajectory, not just count restatements. _Composes with the escalate-to-user-capture idea below._
  - **Design input — error→fix as a first-class memory category (2026-06-15, [AWS AgentCore + build-slides note](../docs/research/2026-06-15-aws-agentcore-memory-and-build-slides.md)):** AWS's build-event slides ("What Do We Store: Facts / **Errors** / Research" + a `memories/errors-and-fixes/` folder) and AgentCore's custom-strategy both treat **"we hit error X → the fix was Y" as its own memory category.** The kit captures this today only as a generic `project`/`reference` fact — no error→fix TYPE with a "what was the fix when we last hit this?" retrieval affordance. Same instinct as the ruflo/memclaw dossier below (trajectory + outcome memory), but more concrete. When designing 55, evaluate an `error`/`fix` (or error-shaped `lesson`) type + the auto-extract rule that recognizes error→fix turns. **Caveat:** a new fact TYPE ripples through the enum + trust + routing + persona logic + the taxonomy in 4 files (`write-fact.mjs`, `auto-extract.mjs`, `mcp-server.mjs`, `import-claude-md.mjs`) — design deliberately, don't bolt on. Overlaps Task 66 (a fix is true until the dep changes — temporal). _Provenance: the user's AWS build-event photos + the AgentCore docs._ **SECOND (stronger) source 2026-06-15 ([project-memory predecessor note](../docs/research/2026-06-15-project-memory-skill-predecessor.md)):** the Claude skill the user USED BEFORE building this kit had `bugs.md` as a first-class file with a clean ready-made schema — **Issue / Root Cause / Solution / Prevention** (dated, chronological). Adopt that quadruple as the error→fix shape; the **Prevention** field is the durable cross-project lesson (→ HABITS/LESSONS), the rest is project-scoped. Error→fix is now confirmed by an actually-used tool, not just a slide._
  - [ ] 55.1 Recurrence/cue detection in auto-extract
  - [ ] 55.2 Promotion to HABITS.md / persona with pattern_count + confidence field
  - [ ] 55.3 Snapshot surfacing + review-queue routing for medium-confidence patterns
  - [ ]* 55.4 Tests — recurrence detection; promotion lands in the right tier; medium-confidence routes to review; over-mutation guard

Our no-loss diff (re-curate → reviewable new output, adopt-or-discard) is OURS, no surveyed product ships it.** **Two unresolved forks (why needs-design):** (1) review-gate vs auto-apply-revertible — the D-169 no-ritual tension (a review queue the user must drain is the anti-pattern; "auto-apply + `cmk forget` to revert" is the field-standard posture we already adopted for persona); (2) raw-transcript input is a PRIVACY surface (`context/transcripts/` is gitignored — the re-curation must emit a SANITIZED output only, never leak raw transcript content into a committed file). **Hard-blocked on Task 66** (D-166: 66 before 95 — 95 consumes 66's validity-window engine). _VERDICT: needs-deeper-design; backlog-no-version-yet, sequenced AFTER 66 (v0.4.4) — likely v0.4.5+ once the two forks are grilled._
  - **🔬 SECOND READY-TEMPLATE — U-Mem's Eliminate/Consolidate/Refine/Retain maintenance prompt (2026-07-01, [U-Mem note](../docs/research/2026-07-01-umem-autonomous-memory.md), D-251).** U-Mem's appendix Fig 12 (the "Memory Refinement Expert" prompt) is a second, independently-published template for this exact batched pass — a verified primary-source alternative to mem0's ADD/UPDATE/DELETE/NONE. Its four objectives map onto our re-curation: **Eliminate** (redundancy — highly-similar memories), **Consolidate** (merge a specific into its general form into one robust unit), **Refine** (generalize imprecise phrasing to raise transferability), **Retain** (keep valuable non-redundant items untouched). Two specifics worth stealing at build time: (a) every extracted memory must **state WHEN it applies (a trigger condition)** — U-Mem enforces this in ALL its extraction prompts; adopting it in OUR auto-extract (not just re-curation) would raise fact transferability today; (b) the explicit note *"a retrieved historical memory that was irrelevant this time is not necessarily useless — evaluate by generalizability"* — a guard against over-eager DELETE. Compare mem0's vs U-Mem's prompt at build time and take the better framing (or merge). _Relates the mem0 gem above (the first template), U-Mem note, the deferred F-D semantic-dedup (gap G5 — reworded-restatement dedup), D-251._
  - **🔬 REVISIT-WHEN-BUILDING — memclaw's fingerprint-dedup-of-rejected (deep-read 2026-06-29; D-223).** A clean pattern for the re-curation pass: memclaw's Forge computes a `fp:v1:sha256(goal+domain+top-entities+step-skeleton)` per candidate and keeps a **`forge_rejected_fingerprints` cooloff table** — when the user rejects a proposed merge/insight, its fingerprint is recorded with a 30-day TTL so the SAME proposal isn't re-surfaced next run. For us (single-user, markdown): a local `rejected-fingerprints.json` (or a tombstone-class marker) keyed on the re-curation candidate's content hash, with a TTL — so a reviewable re-curation pass doesn't re-propose a dedup/merge the user already declined. Closes the "the curation keeps suggesting the same thing I said no to" annoyance. _Relates Task 180 (same fingerprint primitive), the tombstone discipline (rejected ≈ a soft-tombstone), D-223._

The hazard: the smokes run the real `cmk` bin with **no `MEMORY_KIT_USER_DIR` / `CMK_PROJECT_DIR` isolation**, so a wired MUTATING child that someone forgets to add to the skip-set runs **destructively against the real `~/.core-memory-kit`**. Task 72 hit exactly this — `cmk persona import placeholder` round-tripped the maintainer's real persona (backed up + rewritten; caught + cleaned) before the skip-set entry landed. The skip-set is the per-command fix; this task is the structural one: run the whole scaffold smoke with `MEMORY_KIT_USER_DIR` + `CMK_PROJECT_DIR` pointed at a per-test temp dir so even a real mutating command can't touch the host. v0.2.x test-hardening. _Relates D-69, the five-doors "external calls" discipline (a smoke that mutates the host isn't isolated)._

The anti-pattern (conflating "didn't crash" with "works") is now caught both by the checklist rule AND the suite tests.
  - [ ] 116.x **Test-isolation hardening (filed from Task 111's stress gate, D-93).** The 5× stress gate surfaced two PRE-EXISTING concurrency flakes — both pass in isolation, fail only under parallel load: (1) `cli-capture-turn` spawn-latency (FIXED in 111 as a detour — `pollFor` 3s→12s); (2) **`cli-persona-portability` Task-72 rollback flake** (`expected 'ORIGINAL B profile' … got 'NEW imported profile'`, with `r.action==='error'` — so the failure DID trigger + rollback ran, but USER.md wasn't restored). **Investigation (2026-06-08, Task 116 start) RULED OUT the cross-contamination hypothesis:** the fixture IS per-test `mkdtempSync` (not a shared path); `reindex` takes an explicit `userDir` (no `process.env`/homedir fallback); vitest's **default file isolation** prevents cross-file `process.env.MEMORY_KIT_USER_DIR` leakage (the file mutates it at lines 237/264 but that can't reach another isolated file); and `applyBundleAtomic`'s logic is deterministic (insertion-order keys → USER.md backed up before `aaa` fails → `rollbackImport` `renameSync`s the backup over the new USER.md). **Actual diagnosis: a RARE Windows-filesystem-under-load timing race in the `renameSync` backup/restore** — same CLASS as the capture-turn spawn-latency flake (only manifests when `npm run stress` runs 5 vitest processes saturating the machine; 5/5 clean on a quiet re-stress). **✅ FIXED:** `rollbackImport`'s restore did `renameSync(bkp, dest)` where `dest` already holds the half-applied NEW content — renaming ONTO an existing file intermittently throws EPERM/EBUSY on Windows under load, and the **fully-silent catch** then left the un-rolled-back NEW file (= the flake). Replaced with `restoreBackup()` using `copyFileSync` (reliable cross-platform overwrite) + a 5× retry for transient locks; backup removed only after a confirmed copy. 14/14 persona-portability green. Real FS-robustness fix, not test-isolation (isolation was already correct).

- [ ] 141. **[RE-VERDICTED 2026-07-10 (D-311, the trigger-fired walk): phase (a) was ALREADY SHIPPED in v0.3.x (PR #169, HC-8 + install-time ask + README note — D-260 caught the stale re-lane at the v0.4.4 build); phase (b)'s "or the v0.5.0 cut" trigger clause FIRED at the 2026-07-10 tag — verdict: phase (b) stays REJECTED-deferred (the D-162 perf rejection stands, ~10% slower FTS5; HC-8 + the install-time ask remain the npm-12 answer), trigger REFRESHED to: npm-12 GA + the FIRST real native-binding failure report (the fired cut clause dropped). Original disposition preserved below per the decision-trail rule.]** _Original (pre-2026-07-10): [D-253a — TIME-SENSITIVE, re-laned: phase (a) mitigation (doctor HC + README note) rides v0.4.4 (npm 12 ships ~NOW, July 2026); phase (b) node:sqlite migration trigger: npm 12 GA + the first native-binding failure report, or the v0.5.0 cut — whichever first]_ **npm v12 readiness (ships ~July 2026): the install-scripts default flips OFF — our native deps break on fresh installs (D-129).** `better-sqlite3` installs its binding via an install script (`prebuild-install` — already deprecation-warned), and `--with-semantic`'s `onnxruntime-node` does the same: under npm 12 a fresh `npm install -g` silently skips them → search/index crashes at first use. Two phases: **(a) v0.3.x mitigation** — reproduce under npm 11.16 warnings, add a `cmk doctor` HC ("native binding missing — npm 12 blocked the script; run `npm approve-scripts ...`"), README install note, `--with-semantic` runner guidance. **(b) the structural fix** — replace `better-sqlite3` with **`node:sqlite`** (built into Node 22.5+/24: zero native deps, zero install scripts, immune to the class; also ends the reinstall EPERM locked-DLL annoyance). `node:sqlite` supports `loadExtension` for sqlite-vec. The user (2026-06-12): "we can upgrade our node version to latest at any time" — the engines floor is NOT a blocker; spike the sqlite-vec extension load + the better-sqlite3 API-surface migration (index-db/search/semantic-backend callers). No git/URL deps in the kit, so `--allow-git`/`--allow-remote` are no-impact.
  - [x] 141a. **Phase (a) mitigation** — _shipped 2026-06-12 (D-134)._ **UX pivot at task start (the user's steer: "when you install you ask the user, not a secondary command after"):** the original plan made the doctor HC the discovery point; the shipped design makes **`cmk install` probe the binding up front and ask to fix INLINE** (`[Y/n]` → runs the remedy itself); the new **HC-8** is the backstop only. Primary-source corrections to the original spec wording (GitHub changelog 2026-06-09 + npm config docs): the block covers IMPLICIT node-gyp builds (better-sqlite3's exact shape), and `npm approve-scripts` is project-only — **global installs need the `allow-scripts` config** (`npm install -g <pkg> --allow-scripts=<dep>`), which is what the kit emits. `--with-semantic`'s runner passes `--allow-scripts=onnxruntime-node` itself on npm ≥ 11.16 (version-probed, no unknown-config noise on older npm). New module: `native-binding.mjs` (probes distinguish not-installed from installed-but-binding-broken — the semantic loader collapses both).
  - [x] 141b. **Phase (b) `node:sqlite` migration — REJECTED on perf 2026-06-16 (D-162); better-sqlite3 stays.** The three D-147 gates: Spike 1 (node:sqlite ships FTS5 on Node 24) ✅, Spike 2 (sqlite-vec loadExtension) ✅, Spike 3 (perf bake-off) ❌ — CLEAN CI data (`bench-storage.yml`, run 27594513495, noise ±0.3–3.7%) showed **node:sqlite ~10% SLOWER on FTS5 keyword search (1.105×, ±0.8%)** + ~32% slower on incremental reindex; only sqlite-vec KNN tied. Per D-147's no-measurable-regression bar, the migration loses (search latency is paid every query; the npm-12 prompt is one-time). 141a's install-time ask stands as the npm-12 answer. The bench harness + CI workflow are kept (re-run if a future Node release fixes node:sqlite's FTS5 perf). _Was specced below; unblocked, M-sized, slotted v0.3.2 (D-146) — then rejected on the perf gate._ **GATED on TWO spikes that both must pass BEFORE migrating (D-147):** (1) **perf bake-off** — `scripts/bench-storage.mjs` times `node:sqlite` vs `better-sqlite3` on the kit's real hot paths (gated read/search: FTS5 keyword · sqlite-vec cosine · per-read incremental reindex; reported-not-gated: reindexFull · single `cmk remember` write), N≥20× warm, interleaved A/B, same corpus; **bar = NO MEASURABLE REGRESSION (D-147, the user's hardened call): `node:sqlite_p95 ≤ better_p95 × 1.03` per gated read path (3% = harness noise floor, not a budget)**; if it FAILS, 141b does NOT ship and 141a's install-time ask stays the npm-12 answer — *"slower is worse than asking the user y/n on script install… we dont sacrifice the kit's purpose"* (the user). (2) **cross-platform `sqlite-vec` loadExtension spike** (Win/mac/Linux). (3) **`node:sqlite` FTS5-module spike** (added 2026-06-15, Task 153 research): confirm `node:sqlite` ships FTS5 compiled in on all 3 platforms — `better-sqlite3` prebuilts include it by default, `node:sqlite` may NOT (real signal: openclaw #62328 "node:sqlite missing FTS5 module — memory search keyword fallback broken"); the kit's entire keyword layer is FTS5, so if it's absent 141b does NOT ship regardless of perf/vec. Cheapest of the three — run FIRST as the fast-fail gate. Also rides 141b: the Node-engines-floor bump (≥22.5).
  - Estimate: M (b) / S (a) · Depends: none · _v0.3.x; (a) before July — DONE._

UNTESTED composition: each agent spawns its OWN stdio `cmk mcp serve` against the SAME SQLite index - concurrent reads should hold (WAL), but N agents calling `mk_remember` simultaneously is lock-contention territory never exercised (the lock-discipline + better-sqlite3 busy-timeout surface; composes with 141b's node:sqlite migration). Investigate: (a) a live workflow whose agents query mk_search (does recall reach a swarm?); (b) a concurrent-writes stress (N parallel mcp serve processes writing); (c) document the kit-x-workflows story in README/docs if it holds ("your swarm shares one memory"); (d) design question from the squad review (2026-06-12): per-ROLE memory partitions for swarm agents (squad gives each agent a charter.md + history.md alongside shared decisions) - shared facts + per-role histories may beat one undifferentiated pool at swarm scale.
  - **🔬 PRIMARY-SOURCE GEM + STATUS (deep-read 2026-06-28; D-218).** Confirmed a REAL data-loss path, not just theoretical: `appendScratchpadBullet` uses lockless `readFileSync`/`writeFileSync` on the markdown, and the SQLite index has **zero `busy_timeout`** → immediate `SQLITE_BUSY` under N concurrent servers. **mem0** names our exact race (funnel concurrent read-modify-write into ONE batched pass). **letta** ([`orm`](https://github.com/letta-ai/letta)) uses an optimistic `version_id_col` → raises `StaleDataError` so a lost write is LOUD, never silent — the pattern to steal. **Fit nuance:** the DB half needs only a `busy_timeout` (cheap); the MARKDOWN half needs the lock (reuse auto-extract's existing `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` lock-discipline). Patch-shaped. _VERDICT: keep-as-own-task; backlog-no-version-yet, RIDES Task 50 — the cross-agent work multiplies the concurrent servers, so live-test the race THERE (today, with one agent, it's mostly latent). Sequence alongside whichever agent release stresses it._
  - **🔬 REVISIT-WHEN-BUILDING — `songth1ef/nestwork`'s PARTITION-BY-WRITER concurrency (deep-read 2026-06-29; D-227).** A git-native markdown multi-agent memory tool (11★, license-unspecified — patterns not code) solves the concurrent-write race CHEAPER than locks/CRDT/busy-timeout: **each agent OWNS its own directory** (`agents/<host>/<agent-id>/memory.md`) so write paths NEVER overlap → no conflict resolution needed; plus a PreToolUse `git pull --rebase` before write + PostToolUse commit+push after, collapsing the race window to a single op. **For us:** if Task 50 ever has multiple agents writing the SAME shared `context/` concurrently, the cheapest fix may be PARTITION-BY-WRITER (per-agent scratchpad files) rather than locking the shared file — sidesteps the lock entirely for the per-agent surface, keeping locks only for the genuinely-shared facts. A real alternative to the lock-the-shared-file approach (mem0/letta) noted above. _Relates Task 50 (cross-agent — where the partition would live), the lock-discipline; D-227._
  - Estimate: S/M · Depends: none (141b strengthens) · _v0.3.x._

Different output, different surface.   - **🔬 PRIMARY-SOURCE REFERENCE — caura-memclaw's "Forge / Skill Factory" is a WORKING memory→skill-synthesis pipeline (deep-read 2026-06-29; D-223). UPGRADES this task from "maybe too far / no prior art" to "exploratory but with a proven reference design."** memclaw (Apache-2.0) BUILT exactly this (it was vaporware at our 2026-06-12 README review; now production code: `core_api/services/forge/forge_service.py` + `distill_prompt.py` + `fingerprint.py` + a spec/impl-plan). The real pipeline, adapted to OUR single-user + markdown model: **(1) build session traces** + infer outcome from **6 PASSIVE signals — NO user ritual** (contradiction-detected, fact-superseded, repeat-recall=first-answer-failed, terminal-memory-classification "shipped/fixed" vs "blocked/abandoned", cross-agent reuse, optional git-commit/CI hooks tied to session) — this is the D-169-compatible answer to "where's the success signal?"; **(2) cluster** repeated workflows (memclaw uses entity-Jaccard ≥40%; for us = recurrence across SESSIONS/PROJECTS, since we're single-user not a fleet); **(3) gate on volume + diversity** (≥N recurrences before synthesizing — don't make a skill from one-off noise; their "≥3 distinct agents" → our "≥N distinct sessions/projects"); **(4) distill via LLM** (`distill_prompt.py` is a ready template) into a candidate `SKILL.md` with PROVENANCE (`cites:[mem-ids]`, `evidence:"across 4 sessions…"`, `cluster_fingerprint`); **(5) fingerprint** (`fp:v1:sha256(goal+domain+top-entities+step-skeleton)`) + a **rejected-fingerprints cooloff table** (don't re-propose a skill the user already rejected — for us a local `.json` + 30d TTL); **(6) NEVER auto-promote** — candidates stay `candidate` until evidence gates pass (recurrence + convergence + freshness) = EARN-IT, not auto-apply (composes with 151's recurrence-scoring + D-154 post-hoc-revert). **The honest catch:** memclaw is Postgres + multi-tenant + FLEET (its diversity gate is "across agents" — meaningless for one user); we adapt the SHAPE (passive-signal outcomes, fingerprint-dedup, earn-it gates, recurrence-across-sessions), NOT the server machinery. **This makes 180's GO/NO-GO sharper:** the question is no longer "is memory→skill even possible?" (memclaw proved it) but "does a single user generate enough recurrence to clear the earn-it gate, or does the injected persona already cover it?" _Relates Task 97/177 (the passive-outcome-signal gem cracks their open "signal without ritual" gap too), Task 95 (the fingerprint-dedup-rejected pattern), Task 151 (earn-it = recurrence-scoring), D-223._

Already cloned: `research-clones/basic-memory/src/basic_memory/api/v2/routers/project_router.py`. **Cross-project search — `cmk search --all-projects` (promoted from a v0.1.0 deferred one-liner to a real trackable task; the v0.4.3 cold-open made the case, 2026-07-01; D-247).** **The deferral (verbatim, tasks.md v0.1.0 deferred-ideas list):** *"Cross-project search (`cmk search --all-projects`) — v0.2."* It has sat as an un-scoped bullet since v0.1.0 — now a real entry because the Session-3 cold-open showed WHY it matters. **The gap:** `cmk search` is scoped to ONE project's index (`<projectRoot>/context/.index/memory.db`). A query from project B cannot reach a fact captured in project A — even though the user often WANTS that ("how did I solve this in my other project?", "what's my standard X across everything?"). Today the ONLY cross-project channel is (1) the user-tier persona (a curated SUBSET, promoted via `cmk lessons promote`) and (2) injection of that persona at SessionStart. There is no way to SEARCH across all your projects' captured facts. **Why the cold-open makes the case:** in Session 3, the fresh project couldn't SEARCH the user's uv/ruff preferences (they lived in cut-gate19's project tier + the un-indexed persona — Task 182); a true `--all-projects` search would let a new project find a relevant fact from ANY prior project, not just the hand-promoted persona slice. **Design questions to resolve at task-time (this is DESIGN-first):** ☐ how are project index-dbs discovered/registered (a user-tier registry of known project roots? a scan? explicit `cmk project register`)? ☐ merge-search across N sqlite dbs vs. a central user-tier index that mirrors all projects (the latter re-raises the privacy + staleness questions)? ☐ how does it compose with the deferred `cmk persona sync <git-url>` (design §16, 72.2) — is cross-project search a LOCAL-only feature or does it want the synced remote? ☐ ranking across projects (recency? per-project trust? project-relevance?). ☐ privacy: a cross-project result surfaces project A's content in project B's session — is that always wanted, or opt-in per query? **Done when:** a design note (registry vs central-index, the privacy model, the ranking) + a GO/NO-GO; if GO it spawns the build task. NOT a commitment to build in a specific version yet. · Estimate: M (design) + M–L (build, if GO) · Depends: none (but composes with 182 persona-search + the 72.2 persona-sync deferral) · _Relates Task 182 (in-project persona-unsearchable — a PREREQUISITE-adjacent fix; both are "search can't see what the user expects"), Task 65/46 (the search engine this extends), design §16 / 72.2 (`cmk persona sync` — the cross-machine sibling). Provenance: the v0.1.0 deferred-ideas bullet, promoted after the v0.4.3 cold-open surfaced the cross-project recall gap. - [ ] 189. **→ FIRED-WALK VERDICT 2026-07-20 (D-372): its trigger ("triage at the Task 185 sweep") FIRED — 185 shipped — so the triage is due and this is it. LANED v0.7.0 as a RIDER on Task 95**, on the Task-212 precedent (that task was laned as 194's before/after instrumentation): 95 re-curates the corpus, and 189 is exactly the measurement that says whether the re-curation HELPED. Measuring ROI before and after a curation pass is worth far more than measuring it against an un-curated baseline alone, and 95's slot is now committed (D-372), so the dependency is real rather than speculative. **Recurrence = memory ROI — MEASURE how much the kit's memory is actually helping, per project (NEW out-of-the-box idea from the U-Mem harvest; potential differentiator; 2026-07-01; D-251).** **The finding (U-Mem's strongest empirical result):** Pearson **r = 0.888** between task-similarity (how much a new task resembles past ones) and the performance gain memory provides — *"memory is a bet on repetition."* Memory helps most in high-recurrence domains (same codebase / conventions / problem-shape), near-zero for wildly-different one-off work. **The out-of-the-box idea:** no surveyed memory product MEASURES its own recurrence/ROI. The kit could — using the semantic backend it already ships (Task 65, sqlite-vec + embeddings) — compute a per-project "recurrence score" (how similar is this session's work to the captured history?) and (a) TELL the user how much memory is helping them ("your work is highly recurring — memory is paying off" vs "mostly novel this session — memory has less to offer"), and/or (b) ROUTE capture effort toward high-recurrence domains where it pays. This turns U-Mem's *analysis metric* into a *user-facing product feature* — a "your memory is working" signal that's honest (it can say "not much this session") and unique. **⚠️ RESEARCH-then-decide (do NOT commit to build — this needs its own design):** ☐ is a recurrence score ACTIONABLE for a single user, or just a vanity metric? (the honest risk: knowing "memory helped 12%" may not change anything the user does); ☐ can it be computed cheaply from the existing embedding index (AMCS-style average-max-cosine-similarity between new facts and the corpus) without a new pipeline? ☐ does it surface in `cmk doctor` / a `cmk stats` view, or the snapshot? ☐ does routing capture-by-recurrence conflict with "capture everything, it's cheap" (the kit's current stance)? ☐ is this a differentiator worth a lane, or a research-note curiosity? **Done when:** a research note (is the metric actionable + cheap-to-compute on our stack?) + a GO/NO-GO; if GO it spawns a design task with a version lane. NOT a build commitment. · Estimate: S (scoping research note + go/no-go; a build, if GO, is M) · Depends: Task 65 (the semantic/embedding layer any recurrence score reuses) · _v0.5+ candidate (a "measure-your-own-value" feature, past the core loop). **Trigger (D-248): triage at the Task 185 sweep; if GO, lane after Task 65's semantic layer is a stable base to build the AMCS metric on. → TRIAGED at the v0.5.0-cut walk (recorded in the D-307-era sweep entry): the recall-log dependency shipped (Task 190), verdict KEEP as a natural v0.5.x follow-on; annotated here 2026-07-10 (D-311) so the entry stops pointing at a passed sweep.** Provenance: the U-Mem harvest (r=0.888 recurrence↔ROI) + the user's "give us ideas out of the box." - [ ] 223. **→ PRIOR ART (D-374 sweep): READ [`2026-07-07-auto-judged-privacy-prior-art.md`](../docs/research/2026-07-07-auto-judged-privacy-prior-art.md) (~line 47-53) BEFORE deciding the posture.** Its field-wide finding — *regex is structurally incapable of context-dependent PII (names in prose); no maintained JS library does local NER* — PREDICTED this task's live cut-gate failure 12 days before it was observed, and argues AGAINST adding a deterministic L1 pass for this class. **The L3 privacy judge leaves a real name used as a descriptive LABEL in conversational prose ("the fictional Alex Personname") unmasked — evaluate whether label-context names should also redact (surfaced live in the v0.5.1 cut-gate E2; D-323-adjacent).** **What surfaced:** during the v0.5.1 cut-gate Session-3 cold-open, the E2 privacy check confirmed the target property holds — the git-config EMAIL echoed by `uv init`-adjacent tool output was masked to `«EMAIL»` (0 real emails in the committed transcript; original safe in the gitignored `redactions.log`), and the git-config line's name-echo was truncated before it landed. BUT: two occurrences of the bare name "Alex Personname" survived in the committed transcript — NOT from the session's own PII echo, but from META-PROSE captured because this dev repo dogfoods the kit on the driving session too ("git identity set to the fictional Alex Personname"). **Is this a bug?** ARGUABLY NOT: the L3 judge's instruction (`transcript-screen.mjs` `PII_JUDGE_INSTRUCTIONS`) says redact "personal NAMES of real people" — and "the fictional Alex Personname" reads to the judge as a fictional/example name being DISCUSSED, not a real identity being leaked. That is a defensible reading. **But it's a real judgment-boundary worth deciding deliberately:** a name used as a label/example in prose is a genuinely harder case than a raw `user.name X` echo, and a stricter posture (mask ANY name-shaped token regardless of surrounding "fictional"/"example" framing) trades a few false-positive masks for a tighter privacy guarantee. **Done when (research + decision, small):** ☐ decide the posture — keep the current real-person-only reading (record why), OR tighten the judge instruction to mask name-shaped tokens even in label/example context (accepting the FP cost), OR add a deterministic L1 name-pattern pass for the specific "fictional/example <Name>" shape; ☐ if tightening, one transcript-screen test feeding a "the fictional <Name>" prose entry and asserting `«NAME»`; ☐ note the decision in design §6.10. **Reachability:** the security property (real PII from tool OUTPUT doesn't reach the committed transcript) already HOLDS — this is about prose-label names, a narrower surface. · Estimate: S · Depends: none · _**v0.5.x lane** (privacy-polish; NOT a v0.5.1 blocker — the E2 target property passed, this is a judgment-boundary refinement surfaced by the dogfood-on-the-driving-session artifact). Relates Task 148 / ADR-0019 (the L3 judge), `transcript-screen.mjs` `PII_JUDGE_INSTRUCTIONS`, design §6.10, the v0.5.1 cut-gate E2 (the surfacing run)._

- [ ] 221. **Automated cut-gate parity — grow the live-test harness so the manual cut-gate finds less and less (the user's 2026-07-11 directive + the web-research playbook; D-313).** **The recurring failure mode:** unit-green (2836 passing) + integration-broken — the real bugs (D-303 empty `USER_PROMPT`, D-305 `/c:/` path, D-306 UTF-8 BOM, D-298 cron starvation + HC-10 false-green) surfaced ONLY when a human drove the REAL bins against a REAL agent on a REAL project. Google's small/medium/large taxonomy explains WHY: hermetic unit tests are DESIGNED to be blind to process/agent-boundary phenomena; 2836 green + 4 missed bugs aren't in tension. The kit already has the model (`scripts/live-verify.mjs` drives real `claude -p` through real hooks, dev tree shimmed onto PATH, user-tier isolated), but it only covers the persona wedge. **The prioritized playbook (from the [2026-07-11 research report](../docs/research/2026-07-11-faithful-live-test-automation.md), cheapest+highest-recurrence first):** ☐ (1) **fixture corpus of real captured payloads** per agent×platform — generalize the D-306 fix (a real BOM-prefixed Cursor payload was captured via a live stdin probe and committed as a fixture) into a STANDING corpus fed to the real bins as subprocesses; ☐ (2) **property-based "weird input corpus"** (fast-check) for every hook stdin parser — BOM/CRLF/empty/unicode/oversized; ☐ (3) **"watch the watchmen" tests** — seed a broken state, assert the health check reports UNHEALTHY (the HC-10 D-298 test IS this pattern; generalize to every HC that could false-green); ☐ (4) **fault-inject kill-mid-run** — `spawnSync` + timed SIGTERM to kill a cron/distill at 80% and assert forward progress (the Task-204 resumability property, live — this task's `live-distill-probe` shape made permanent); ☐ (5) **real-OS matrix** running the fixture-replay tests (would've caught `/c:/` structurally); ☐ (6) broaden `live-verify.mjs` past the persona wedge (LOWEST priority — most expensive, live-model-dependent). **Explicit non-goals (from the research):** NO chaos-engineering platform (Litmus/Chaos Mesh overkill for a single-machine CLI); NO exact-match assertions on live LLM output (presence-based is correct, already done). **Done when:** items (1)–(4) ship as `npm run` scripts wired appropriately (the deterministic ones into `npm test`; the live-model ones on-demand like `live-verify`), each mapped to the bug class it prevents recurring; a short `docs/process/live-test-playbook.md` records which harness covers which class. · Estimate: L (staged — (3)+(4) are cheap and land first; (1)+(2) next; (5)+(6) as capacity allows) · Depends: none · _**v0.5.x lane (process/quality investment; the user's standing directive makes it committed, not trigger-gated — "so we don't wait for the cut-gate to find problems"). Staged: the cheap high-yield items ((3) watch-the-watchmen, (4) fault-inject) ride the tasks that surface each class (203/204 already added the first of each); the corpus + property-based + matrix items are their own PRs.** Relates the [2026-07-11 research report](../docs/research/2026-07-11-faithful-live-test-automation.md), `scripts/live-verify.mjs` (the model), D-303/D-305/D-306/D-298 (the bug classes it targets), CLAUDE.md "Live-test toward cut-gate parity" (the binding rule this operationalizes), D-313._
  - ☐ **(7) cut-gate §6 E3 script fix — the positive-dampen path is unexercised (surfaced live in the v0.5.1 cut-gate).** E3's failure-turn (`uv run pytest tests/test_nonexistent.py`) produced NO `trust-signals.log` delta — CORRECTLY, because the learn-loop dampens only the ids the model recently SEARCHED (tight attribution, `judge-signals.mjs`), and the cold-open did ZERO in-chat searches (recall.log: inject 2, search 0), so the failure had nothing to attribute to. The E3 script therefore only ever proves the anti-false-positive guard + the recall-attribution log, never a POSITIVE dampen. Fix: the E3 step must script a **search-then-contradict** scenario — (a) ask a question that triggers an in-chat `mk_search` (logs a `source:search` line), THEN (b) a turn whose outcome contradicts a recalled fact (a failed command in the domain of the searched fact, or a `PREDICTION:MISS`) — so the judge has searched ids to dampen and `trust-signals.log` gets a real delta. Until then, E3 in the cut-gate is honestly "loop-plumbing confirmed, positive-signal deferred." _Provenance: the v0.5.1 cut-gate E3 run; relates `judge-signals.mjs` (the tight-attribution rule), `recall-log.mjs`, Task 194 (the ranking this signal feeds)._

- [ ] 228. **Proactive-memory connectors — GO/NO-GO research ADR: does the kit ingest context that never passed through a chat? (the OpenWiki Brains class; from the 2026-07-12 market sweep; D-326).** **The question:** LangChain's OpenWiki Brains (2026-07-10) frames built-in memory as REACTIVE (remembers what you tell the agent / what it infers from conversations — the kit's class) and adds PROACTIVE memory: connectors (Gmail, Notion, git, X, HN, web search) pull external context into a local markdown wiki on a schedule. Their roadmap explicitly names "Claude/Codex local sessions" as a future connector — i.e. they are moving INTO the kit's territory; this ADR decides whether the kit moves the other way. **The honest starting position (record it in the ADR either way):** the clearly IN-THEME cut is **other-agents' local session histories** — which Task 225 already covers (Claude Code) and the cross-agent adapters extend naturally (Kiro/Cursor/Codex session stores as import sources = a 225 tail, not a new paradigm). External SaaS connectors (Gmail/Notion/X) are a NEW security surface (credentialed ingestion + third-party content straight into committed tiers) and likely scope creep for a local-first coding-agent kit — but decide it deliberately, not by drift. **Done when (research + ADR only, no build):** ☐ survey the field (OpenWiki's connector architecture + at least 2 others); ☐ the ADR gives a per-connector-class verdict (agent-session stores / git-derived / external SaaS) with the security analysis (every ingested byte must route through screenBeforeCommittedWrite + the privacy judge — non-negotiable); ☐ a GO on any class spawns a build task laned on cut; a NO-GO records the named re-visit trigger. · Estimate: M (research) · Depends: none · _**v0.7.0 rider (D-326)** — runs alongside the team-layer headline (both are "memory beyond your own sessions" breadth). Relates Task 225 (the in-theme cut it generalizes), Task 174 (git-history backfill — a proactive connector the kit already planned), the [2026-07-12 sweep note](../docs/research/2026-07-12-day-one-memory-market-sweep.md), D-326._

  - **GRILLED 2026-08-02 — design RATIFIED (D-414); canonical contract in design §24; outward research fresh-read per the unconditional prior-art rule ([viewer survey note](../docs/research/2026-08-02-memory-viewer-prior-art.md)).** The 8 points, terse (full detail §24.1): (1) ephemeral localhost server — loopback-only, free port, auto-open, Ctrl-C ends it, NO daemon (the datasette lifecycle, against the field's resident-daemon complaint class); (2) API-first — JSON + HTML from the same routes, the server doubles as the kit's local read API; (3) read-only STRUCTURALLY (no write route mounted; delete demand answered with copy-able `cmk forget/trust <id>` beside every fact); (4) five wave-1 views — search-first landing with pinned health strip · fact detail · kit-semantic graph · health · **decisions journal** (the user's addition: evolution visible, superseded struck); (5) all three tiers, P/L/U badges; (6) manual refresh + per-view freshness labels (SSE = Task 259, filed with trigger); (7) zero new deps — node stdlib http + one committed HTML + vanilla JS + existing FTS; (8) deferred stated: timeline, conflict-queue UI, stats page.
  - **Build sub-tasks (wave 1):** ☑ 255.1 server core — `cmk view` verb, loopback bind + refusal, free port, auto-open via platform-commands, graceful shutdown, no-scaffold guard on uninstalled projects (S) — _shipped: `packages/cli/src/viewer.mjs` (the deep module, one public `startViewer` boundary), `openBrowserCommand` added to `platform-commands.mjs`, `runView` + the verb in `subcommands.mjs`, `view` classified CLI-only in the parity guard. Two hardenings beyond the filed text, both from the prior-art survey: a `Host:`-header DNS-rebinding guard (a loopback BIND alone does not stop a hostile page resolving its own domain to 127.0.0.1) and a `default-src 'none'` CSP that makes the offline/no-CDN rule fail loudly instead of silently._; ☑ 255.2 JSON API routes — facts/search/fact-detail/graph/health/decisions over the existing fact-store + FTS + health-log + edges readers (M) — _shipped: five routes, every one a reader over an EXISTING module (`search()` for FTS, `traverseLinks`/`supersessionChain` for edges, `runDoctor` + `activeWarnings` for health, the journal parser for decisions) — no threshold is re-derived in the viewer, so the health strip cannot disagree with the 250 whisper. Three shared readers grew rather than being re-rolled inline: `decisions-journal.parseDecisionsJournal` (search's `--scope decisions` backend now takes it too — one parser, was about to become three), `fact-store.eachSupersededFact` (graph-index's private archive walk now takes it), and `rich-fact.parseRichFactBody` (the inverse of the writer, beside the writer). The facts route deliberately calls `search()` WITHOUT `projectRoot`, which is what suppresses the recall-log append — browse traffic in `recall.log` would corrupt the ADR-0024 fire-rate._; ☑ 255.3 the HTML page — five views, vanilla JS consuming the API, tier badges, freshness labels, copy commands (M) — _shipped: `packages/cli/src/viewer-page.html`, one file, inline CSS+JS, light/dark, zero external references (asserted, and the response CSP `default-src 'none'` makes a stray one fail loudly). Landing is search-first with the pinned health strip; fact detail carries Why/How + supersession chips + both edge directions + three copy-able commands; the graph is a hand-rolled deterministic force layout in SVG (trust = colour, supersession = the one directed arrow, anchors = accent hubs); health renders the 14 checks + active warnings with copy-able fixes; decisions strikes retracted entries. **Honest limit:** the suite compiles the inline script (a syntax error would ship a blank page) but the kit has no DOM environment — adding one is a new dep — so VISUAL/interaction correctness needs the user's manual browser session._; ☑ 255.4 tests — route contracts (five doors), loopback refusal, read-only structural test (no write route responds), empty/uninstalled project, live-verify driving the REAL bin + real HTTP requests (S–M) — _shipped: `tests/cli-viewer.test.js` (42, all over REAL HTTP on an ephemeral port — nothing reaches an internal helper) + `scripts/live-verify-viewer.mjs` / `npm run live-verify:viewer` (19 checks, real bin as a subprocess, real corpus written by the real `cmk remember`/`digest`). The read-only test is a MATRIX (6 methods × routes, raw HTTP because undici refuses to send TRACE) and the recall-log assertion is Door 5 in the negative. Two budget pairs registered in `validate-budget-pairs`. **The live-verify earns its keep on four properties no in-process test can see:** the process STAYS ALIVE after commander's action returns, the committed HTML resolves from the installed tree, SIGINT releases the port, and the CLI refusals are exit-2-with-a-reason rather than exceptions._; ☑ 255.5 docs walk — CLI.md verb entry, README capability line, CHANGELOG, glossary, design §24 refinements from build (S) — _the full source-of-truth-table walk, verdict per doc: **TOUCHED** — design.md (new **§24.1.1** "what wave 1 settled at BUILD time", additive; the eight ratified points untouched) · CLI.md (the verb) · README.md + packages/cli/README.md (capability line + CLI-table row, both) · CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` · glossary (**Memory viewer** + **Freshness label**) · **SECURITY.md** (a threat-surface row — a local listener over the user tier is a real surface and the table had no row for it) · **SYSTEM-MAP.md** (a new organ: `INSPECT`, plus its `displayed-by` reader edge and its `observe-without-disturbing` constraint) · specs/tasks.md. **N/A, with reason** — MCP.md (no tool added, and deliberately: `view` is classified CLI-only in the parity guard — the model already has every read as an MCP tool and cannot open a URL) · memory-lifecycle-map.md (nothing about write/cap/graduate/recall changed) · ARCHITECTURE.md (no new layer — a read surface over Layer 5) · HEALTH-CHECKS.md (no new HC) · DOCUMENTATION-MAP.md (no new registered doc) · QUICKSTART/BOOTSTRAP (not an install step) · per-agent docs CLAUDE-CODE/KIRO/CURSOR/CODEX (agent-agnostic verb, no per-agent wiring) · RELEASE-PLAN (the v0.6.4 lane already names 255 as the headline; the cut entry is the ship step) · ADRs (no new architectural decision — §24 is the ratified record). The counts validator passes on the live registry at 43 verbs._ **Done when:** all five sub-boxes; `npm test` + stress green (spawn/server surface — the gate binds); live-verify proves the real bin serves all five views read-only on a real corpus; the docs walk states its verdicts.
  - **REVIEW ROUND (the D-390 two-pass split's second pass — 1 Blocking + 3 Important + 8 Minor, every one reproduced or confirmed by the reviewer, all fixed on-branch).** The security core HELD under an independent pass: XSS ruled out by full sink enumeration plus a real seeded payload, the read-only method×route matrix complete, loopback/Host/traversal all clean. What it caught:
    - **B1 (Blocking) — the pinned strip printed a green all-clear over a RED doctor table.** The strip read only the health LOG, and a hook that is never registered never runs, so it never logs: remove the hooks block and `HC-1` FAILs while the log stays silent and the most prominent line in the UI says "capture … reporting fine". The HC-10 false-green class, on the surface a user trusts most. **Fixed per the lead's arbitration:** the full route folds `fail`/`warn` into the strip (FAIL outranks everything and names the failing check ids; warn-only reads amber), and `?strip=1` — which structurally cannot see an unregistered hook — has its positive wording bounded to what the log proves ("no failures recorded (quick check)"), never a claim about capture/recall/index. The health view repaints the pinned line from its doctor-informed strip. Pinned by a unit test AND by the live-verify check that **replaced M7's tautological one** (which had compared a clean strip against a clean doctor line — nothing to nothing): it now unwires the hooks in the sandbox and demands the strip follow, plus asserts the log is silent, which is the whole reason the fold was needed.
    - **I1 + M8 — the fact page's copy commands were fiction in two directions.** On an ARCHIVED fact all three were no-ops against a record already out of the live corpus; on a LIVE fact `cmk forget <id>` stopped at an interactive prompt (the paste appeared to do nothing) and the trust command echoed the CURRENT level, so running it changed nothing. Fixed: archived facts get `commands: null` + a `state_note` linking their successor; live facts get `cmk forget <id> --yes` (the human choosing to paste and run in their own shell IS the ADR-0018 confirmation) and a trust **template** over the whole ladder with the current level marked and no command offered for it.
    - **I2 — the read-only guards compared paths only**, so the viewer could have rewritten every byte of every fact and the assertion would still pass. Now snapshots path + size + mtime + SHA-256 per file, and the matrix's over-mutation guard compares row CONTENT rather than a count.
    - **I3 — no structural guard against HTML-parsing sinks.** The page is XSS-safe by discipline (`textContent`/`createElement` everywhere), which one careless edit undoes. A test now scans the inline script for `innerHTML`/`outerHTML`/`insertAdjacentHTML`/`document.write`/`srcdoc`/`setHTML`/`createContextualFragment` and fails on any occurrence — discipline converted to enforcement, recorded in design §24.1.1.
    - **Minors:** M1 archived `source_file` now formed by the indexer's own rule (tier-prefixed, `context/memory/archive/superseded/<id>.md`) · M2 the wire field `superseded` → **`from_archive`** (it collided with the graph node's `superseded`, which means "has a successor"; renamed while the API still has no consumers) · M3 SECURITY.md now says escaping is the XSS defense and the CSP guarantees offline-ness, plus `frame-ancestors 'none'` added to the real header (it does not inherit from `default-src`) · M4 `pendingQueues` distinguishes unreadable from zero — an unreadable queue is amber "unknown", never a silent green · M5 the index-refresh failure line carries the `cmk reindex --full` hint, matching `cmk search` · M6 the live-verify traversal check moved to a RAW SOCKET, because every Node HTTP client normalizes `..` out of the path before sending — the old check was asking about `/package.json`, a question the server was never going to answer wrongly · M7 folded into B1 · M8 folded into I1.
    - **Also from the review's honesty items:** the `/api/graph` live check was vacuous (`edges.every(...)` over an empty array), so the sandbox now seeds a real supersession and the check demands `edges.length > 0` plus the directed old→new edge; and §24.1.1's "each request re-runs the boot reindex" was imprecise — true only for the three DB-backed routes, now stated as such.
