- [x] 261. _shipped 2026-08-04 (v0.6.5, held-unpublished lane)._ **BLOCKER — the semantic vector index is keyed by a rowid that reindex reassigns, so 15% of facts hold another fact's embedding (found 2026-08-04 while researching Task 262; D-421).** **The symptom, user-visible:** `cmk search "never overwrite backup directories" --mode semantic` returns five facts about gate tests, DECISIONS.md, hook ceilings, tombstones and release workflow — **none about backups**, while the real fact (`P-BYPATXDD`) exists. **The measurement (this repo's live index, reproduced independently after correcting a first bad read):** 2,332 vec rows, **1,972 distinct vectors — 360 rows (15.4%) carry a byte-identical embedding belonging to a DIFFERENT fact.** Sampled pairs are semantically unrelated ("CHECKPOINT — kiro-cli FULLY WORKS" ⟷ "The snapshot builder must not import the sqlite index"), which rules out the innocent explanation (vectors are content-addressed by `sha256(model+body)`, so identical text SHOULD share a vector — these texts are not identical). **Falsified per D-366 before filing:** `embedding_cache` holds 9,697 rows with 9,697 DISTINCT vectors → the embedder is fine; `vec_observations_rowids` has 2,332 unique `(chunk_id, chunk_offset)` slots → not a read artifact; the first count (447 dups / 88 all-zero) was MY OWN bad mapping assuming sequential packing — redone against the real `chunk_id`/`chunk_offset` columns it is 360 dups / 0 zeros, matching the independent finding exactly. **Root cause, traced to the line:** `semantic-backend.mjs:13` documents the assumption as if it were safe — *"The vec table mirrors `observations` rowids"* — but `index-rebuild.mjs:382` inserts observations WITHOUT an explicit rowid (SQLite auto-assigns), and `reindexFull` (`:682-695`) drops `observations` / `observations_fts` / `transcript_chunks` / `files` / `edges` **but NOT `vec_observations`**. So a rebuild reassigns every rowid while the vec table keeps the old mapping, and the skip guard at `semantic-backend.mjs:317` (`present && plan.cached → continue`) reads the stale row as correct and never replaces it. `:454` then joins `observations o ON o.rowid = m.rowid` and returns the wrong fact. **`cmk reindex --full` — the documented repair command, the one the troubleshooting skill prescribes — is therefore the CAUSE, and there is no user-facing repair today.** **Why every test missed it:** a FRESH index assigns rowids 1..N in insert order, so vectors line up perfectly; the corruption only appears on a rebuild over an EXISTING index, which no test performs and which is exactly how a real corpus evolves. The unit-green ≠ works-on-real-input class, on the kit's headline recall feature. **The fix — SHIPPED 2026-08-04 (design §9.3.2):** ☑ (a) the lockstep-drop was SUPERSEDED by (b) rather than shipped as written — with the mapping keyed by a stable id, dropping the vec tables on rebuild is neither needed for correctness nor desirable (it discards work that is free to keep); `reindexFull` instead CLEARS the keying marker so the next sync force-rebuilds, which is what (c) needs. The per-path `DELETE` needs nothing at all: the key is the fact's id, which a per-file re-index does not change. ☑ (b) **the real fix** — `vec_map(scope, key1, key2, vec_rowid, content_sha)` keyed on `observations.id` / `(source_file, chunk_idx)`, allocated explicitly and never auto-assigned; `:454`'s join moves through it. `content_sha` also made the skip guard sound, which fixed a SECOND live bug found while red-testing (a body edited to text already in the cache kept its stale vector forever). Plus an orphan sweep, so a torn state heals instead of answering wrongly. ☑ (c) **repair, automatic** — a `vec_meta.map_version` generation marker resets any index written under the old keying on its next sync, with zero model calls; `reindexFull` clears the marker, so `cmk reindex --full` / `cmk repair --index` now cure what they used to cause. ☑ (d) **HC-15** — `verifyVectorMapping()` samples 50 live facts, re-derives `sha256(model
body)` from the body and byte-compares the cached vector against what is physically in the slot (independent of the map's own claim); no embedder loaded. ☑ (e) **the AGED-CORPUS harness** (added mid-task by the user's call — the bug was a symptom of a testing gap): every live check we had verified FRESH state and none verified AGED state, which is why the cut-gate's real semantic-recall stage passed legitimately while 86.7% of the corpus was wrong. [`scripts/lib/aged-corpus.mjs`](../scripts/lib/aged-corpus.mjs) is a reusable `build → mutate → rebuild → assert` seam driving the REAL bins (write/expire/forget/supersede/unlink + `reindex --boot` + `reindex --full`), wired at 2 cycles into the always-on suite and 3 into a new `npm run live-verify:semantic` — which also closes the separate gap that semantic search had NO automated live coverage at all. Verified RED on the pre-fix build, on the AGED path, while the FRESH baseline passed in the same run. Design §17.14. It found two further bugs of the same family on its first run (the `superseded_by` FK aborting a rebuild; `meta.edges_built_at` outliving the dropped `edges` table) — both fixed here, D-423. **Done when:** the regression test builds an index, mutates + rebuilds it, and asserts every vec row still matches its own fact's cached vector (red before the fix); a real semantic search for a distinctive phrase returns the right fact on THIS corpus; and the docs stop telling users to run the command that breaks it. · Estimate: M · Depends: none — **blocks Task 262** · _**v0.6.5 lane (the release is CUT but DELIBERATELY UNPUBLISHED — the user's call 2026-08-04, "hold and ship them together"; nothing is public, so the fix lands INSIDE 0.6.5 rather than becoming its own version).** Relates D-421, Task 262 (blocked on this), ADR-0002 (the index is rebuildable derived state — which is exactly why the fix is cheap), the "unit-green ≠ works-on-real-input" rule._

- [ ] 268. **The viewer REDESIGN — the structural direction, not a third coat of paint (the user's verdict on shipped v0.6.5: "still ugly", the third time; filed 2026-08-05, D-425).** **Why a redesign and not another pass:** Task 260 shipped eight ranked improvements and the answer was still no. Read honestly, both passes tuned colour and spacing on a structure that is a settings dialog — and the [design-direction memo](../docs/research/2026-08-04-viewer-visual-direction-memo.md) (a 5-domain survey synthesized against our actual source, with a palette validator run on our real hexes) says so with measurements: **max type anywhere is 16px (total range 1.45×)** so nothing on the page is big; **50 identical rounded cards** where every craft-tier reference (Primer, Sentry, Grafana, NetNewsWire, Linear) uses rows with shared boundaries because cards don't share edges; **the landing shows 50 of ~2,300 facts (2.2%) and never states its own scale**, so the most impressive true thing about the product is never on screen; and the palette was **tuned as text colour then reused as data colour** — two different objectives, nobody owned the seam, and the validator FAILS our own trust hues for chroma and CVD separation in both themes (a full-colour-vision viewer cannot reliably tell medium from low trust). **The ratified position — "an instrument panel over a warm archive":** keep the warm light identity for everything you READ; give the product exactly ONE permanently dark, permanently saturated region — the graph — as the hero object. Not dark-first (the dullness is polarity-invariant: dark cards are still 50 identical boxes; this is a reading surface; and dark-first makes us a Linear clone while warm-light-plus-one-dark-canvas is a position nobody in the reference class holds). **The build:** ☑ (1) **rows, not cards** — one continuous surface, hairline dividers, hover as the affordance, metadata right-aligned in tabular figures (the user chose this explicitly at the 2026-08-04 grill); ☑ (2) **a display type tier + negative tracking ramp** — the memo calls it "the highest-leverage looks-designed change available with system-ui and no font file", and we currently have no large type to hang it on; ☑ (3) **an overview that states the corpus** — count, tier split, a recency strip — so 2,300 facts stop being invisible; ☑ (4) **the graph as a dark hero**: warm-dark canvas `#221f1b` in BOTH page modes (Warp's hue family, the inverse of our own ground — NOT graphify's navy, which is their product choice layered on cosmos.gl's neutral default), degree-scaled repulsion (dandelions are a direct function of it — a constant charge structurally cannot produce them), uncapped `radius = f(degree)`, **hue = community capped at 3 + Other** (arithmetic, not timidity: the validator caps an all-pairs form at 3 slots on a dark surface; graphify's 7-9 saturated colours are not honestly shippable under AA), **trust moves to a rim-arc** — shape not colour, so it is CVD-immune and fixes the a11y gap for free; ☑ (5) a **filter/legend/count rail** (mandatory anyway: identity is never colour-alone for ≥2 series) that also states corpus size so a sparse canvas stops reading as empty; ☑ (6) **detail panel on selection only** — an always-visible empty panel is the single most self-inflicted cause of looking under-populated. **Constraints UNCHANGED:** one static HTML file, inline vanilla CSS/JS, zero dependencies, fully offline, system fonts, read-only, WCAG AA per design §24.1.2 (now a written contract with a ratio-computing test). **The honest caveat that must be stated to the user before they judge it:** the graph will still be SPARSE until Task 262 (write-time linking) ships — 4% of facts carry a link today, which is a data property no CSS fixes. Density arrives with 262; this task makes the graph *look* like an instrument rather than a scatter. **Done when:** the six land, gates green (suite + stress + live-verify:viewer unchanged — this must not touch behaviour), before/after screenshots re-shot, and **the user says it is good** — the criterion transferred from 260, which no test can assert. · Estimate: L · Depends: 260 shipped (its pass is the floor this builds on); pairs with 262 for graph density · _**v0.6.6 lane, as the visual headline alongside Task 262** (one differentiator per minor: 262 gives the memory structure, 268 makes it visible). Relates the [direction memo](../docs/research/2026-08-04-viewer-visual-direction-memo.md) (the spec), the [prior-art survey](../docs/research/2026-08-03-viewer-visual-design.md), Task 260 (superseded), Task 262 (density), Task 259 (live-refresh, same surface), design §24, D-425._
  - **Direction REVISED 2026-08-08 (D-432) — read the "warm light identity" prose above as the ORIGINAL position, not the current one.** Dark is now the DEFAULT theme and the warm-light palette is the fully-supported second theme (served on an explicit `data-theme="light"` — i.e. the reader pressed the button; see the rider below). The new evidence that reopened D-425: three consecutive dark-preference verdicts from the user, ending in a commissioned dark visual pass. Build items (1)(2)(3)(5)(6) are unaffected; item (4) still holds — the graph is still the dark hero, now by being DEEPER than the page (`--g-canvas: #131110` under `--ground: #1a1714`) and by holding the only dense saturation, rather than by being the only dark region. Delivered as a SKIN PORT of the commissioned reference onto the reviewed page, not a file swap: the five review fixes on this branch (viewport-sized instrument · honest-totals `readTotal` · the `Segoe UI Variable` font contract · peek-before-legend · no rail `max-height`) are all preserved, and two reference tokens were adjusted to hold the AA and ORDERED-ladder contracts. See D-432 + design §24.1.2.
- [ ] 269. **Paging + search across the views — you can currently reach 200 of 2,300 facts and 200 of 2,502 decisions, and there is no way to ask for the 201st (filed 2026-08-06, the user's question during the 268 integration: "can't we do just a few, add pages instead of a long list, and search in all of them?").** **The measurement, taken on the real corpus:** `/api/facts` and `/api/decisions` both honour a `?limit=` clamped at `VIEWER_MAX_LIMIT = 200` (default 50) — and **the viewer has no `offset`, `cursor`, or `page` parameter at all** (a grep for any of the four across `viewer.mjs` returns zero). So the ceiling is not "the page is long", it is **91% of the fact corpus and 92% of the decision journal are unreachable through the viewer by any URL**. `/api/decisions` already reports `{count: 200, total: 2502, truncated: true}` — it *knows* it is hiding 2,302 entries and says so in the envelope, and there is nothing the user can do with that fact. **Related asymmetry found in the same check:** the facts envelope carries no `total` at all (keys are `view/generated_at/took_ms/mode/query/tier/limit/clamped/count/facts`), so the facts view structurally cannot state its own scale — which is the same gap Task 268 item (3) is trying to close visually, one layer down. **Health is NOT part of this** — it returns 15 checks, which is a complete list, not a truncated one; paginating it would be ceremony. **The build:** ☐ (1) a **cursor or offset parameter** on `/api/facts` + `/api/decisions`, with the existing clamp preserved (this is the whole functional fix — everything else is affordance); ☑ (2) `total` in the **facts** envelope, so both list views report the same shape and "showing 50 of 2,300" is derivable rather than guessed — **PULLED FORWARD into Task 268 (2026-08-06)**: the redesign promoted a 56px figure to the top of the page and there was no honest number to put in it (browse borrowed `/api/graph`'s differently-filtered `fact_count` at the cost of a second route per page-load; search showed the page size, so a 500-hit query read "50 matches"). Both modes now state `total` — browse under the row query's own filters, search via the shared `countKeywordMatches`. The measurement in this entry's preamble ("the facts envelope carries no `total` at all") is preserved as the state at filing; ☐ (3) **pager affordance** in the page — prev/next over a stable sort, not infinite scroll (a memory archive is a thing you navigate deliberately, and infinite scroll makes a position unciteable); ☐ (4) **search on decisions** reusing the facts search input rather than a second one — the journal is 2,502 entries and searching it is the only realistic way in; ☐ (5) the pager and the search **compose** — searching narrows, paging traverses the remainder, and a page position survives a query change predictably. **Design note to settle during the build:** with 2,300 facts nobody pages to 46, so search is the primary traversal and paging is the honest fallback for "show me everything"; build it in that order of importance rather than treating them as equals. **Done when:** the five land, `live-verify:viewer` extended to assert a second page returns *different* records than the first (the assertion that would have caught this class), the read-only guarantee unchanged (no new method, no write route), and the envelope shapes documented. · Estimate: M · Depends: Task 268 merged (same file; a functional change stacked on an unmerged skin PR is a conflict for no benefit) · _**v0.6.6 lane, riding with Task 268 as the functional half of the same surface.** Relates Task 268 (item 3 states the corpus visually; this makes it reachable), Task 259 (live-refresh, same surface), design §24._
  - **RIDER SHIPPED 2026-08-08 (D-440) — the theme toggle, which is what makes D-432 true for the reader who reported it.** The dark port expressed "default" as `prefers-color-scheme`, so on a light-preferring machine the page was byte-identical to the pre-redesign one and the verdict was *"looks the same to me"*; the `[data-theme]` hooks existed but nothing ever stamped the attribute. Now: a pre-paint `<head>` stamp (stored choice → else **dark**, never the system preference) plus a **Light / Dark button in the header chrome**, persisted to `localStorage`. The sheet went from four root blocks to **two**, each palette written once, and the duplicate-copy drift guard was retargeted into a stronger one (the light override is checked against the DARK palette itself, and every theme-varying token must appear exactly twice). Suite + lint + `live-verify:viewer` green; the rendered page is still the user's call in a browser. See D-440, design §24.1.2.

- [ ] 270. **`writeFact` accepts an explicitly-supplied id it can never find again — a durable file lands on disk and is invisible to every DB-backed route (found live 2026-08-06 during the Task-268 fix pass; D-427).** **The mechanism, CONFIRMED on a real corpus:** the implementer's test fixture used `P-5678ABCD` — `8` is outside the kit's base32 alphabet — and `writeFact` **accepted it and returned `action: 'created'` with a real fact file on disk**; then `index-rebuild.mjs:247` skipped it at sync time (`'invalid or missing id'`), so the fact was unreachable through search, the viewer, the graph, and every other DB-backed surface. Its observed symptom was a wrong `total` (3 instead of 4) — i.e. the failure presents as a COUNTING discrepancy, not as an error, which is why nothing ever surfaced it. This is the worst shape a durability product can have: the write path says yes, the read path says no such fact, and no error fires anywhere between them. `validate-test-ids` catches the fixture class in OUR tests at `npm test` — it does nothing for a real caller (an import script, a restore path, an MCP client passing ids through). **The unconfirmed half, stated honestly:** a repair path exists (`FACT_ID_REPAIRED`, D-394) and **did not fire** across the implementer's two viewer requests against the orphaned fact — whether it never matches this shape or just wasn't reached was not chased; settling that is sub-task 1. **The build:** ☐ (1) trace why `FACT_ID_REPAIRED` didn't fire on an alphabet-invalid explicit id (it may only handle a different corruption shape — if it DOES cover this, the fix may be a wiring gap, not new code); a reject that breaks restore is worse than the bug); ☐ (3) a doctor/HC angle: count fact files whose id fails `ID_PATTERN` or which exist on disk but not in the index — the on-disk-but-invisible population is exactly what a health check is for (compose with HC-15's index-integrity framing, don't duplicate it); ☐ (4) tests walking the five doors on the write boundary: the response door (what does `writeFact` return for an invalid explicit id), the state door (file created or not), and the over-mutation guard (valid neighbours untouched). **Done when:** the four land, the repair-path question has a written answer either way, and a live probe shows an invalid-id write either refused or repaired — never silently orphaned. · Estimate: S · Depends: none (261 shipped the index side; this is the write side) · _**v0.6.6 lane** — small, fully diagnosed, and in the durability core (the D-366 class: a fact that cannot be found is indistinguishable from a lost fact, except this time the write path manufactured it). Relates Task 261 (the index half), D-394 (`FACT_ID_REPAIRED`), D-427, `scripts/validate-test-ids.mjs` (the same alphabet, enforced one layer too late for real callers)._

- [ ] 279. **The live-verify wedge gate is ~50% flaky on Phase A — measured, attributed to harness/LLM nondeterminism, and currently nobody's deliverable (found during 262's finishing batch 2026-08-08; filed 2026-08-09, D-438).** **The measurement:** five clean serial live-verify samples (no concurrent jobs) split PASS/FAIL on the same missing rule ("run ruff before committing") **in BOTH flag conditions** — linking ON failed 3/4 and passed 1/4, linking OFF produced both a 2/2 PASS and a 1/2 FAIL — and the mechanical proof (zero fact files in the wedge sandboxes; the flag's one call site unreachable without a fact create) excludes any 262 involvement. So the wedge gate itself is a coin-flip on whether the LLM emits the ruff rule, which means: (a) every future branch that runs live-verify pays a ~50% false-alarm rate on this phase, (b) a REAL regression in rule capture would hide inside the noise, and (c) the D-422/263 principle (the live gates are the ratchet) is undermined at exactly its most behavioral gate. **The build (small, judgment-bearing):** ☐ characterize — N clean samples on unchanged main to establish the true base rate (the 5-sample matrix is in the [baseline note](../docs/research/2026-08-08-linking-benchmark-baseline.md) §7 context); ☐ decide the fix class — (i) prompt-hardening the wedge turn so the rule emission is reliable, (ii) presence-based assertion relaxation (assert A rule landed, not THE rule — weakens the gate, needs justification), (iii) retry-once-on-miss with both attempts logged (the D-68 always-log discipline), or (iv) a fake-agent deterministic variant for CI with the live variant kept manual (the Task 263 pattern); ☐ whichever ships, the gate's flake rate gets re-measured and recorded; ☐ the failure output must name the missing rule verbatim (it does today — keep it). **Done when:** the wedge gate's false-alarm rate is <5% measured over ≥10 runs, with no real-regression blindness introduced (a seeded capture-breaking mutation must still fail it). · Estimate: S · Depends: none · _**v0.7 lane** (the live-verify surface is Task 263's standing ratchet; this is its first measured reliability defect). So the argument is NOT "users skip the opt-ins therefore default them on" — it is the sharper one: **a feature being ON is not enough; it can be silently broken, which is what the 261 health check + aged-corpus harness address.** The REAL question this leaves is only about the NEW-user first-run: `cmk install` today enables neither semantic recall (a ~260 MB embedder — bare install is keyword-only) nor scheduled maintenance (`register-crons` is a separate command install never calls), and a new user must know the flags exist. [Y/n]" — install already asks one question, this is consistent) with a decline-fallback offer when a search returns nothing useful; (c) default-on (rejected-leaning for semantic — silent 260 MB on `npm install -g` is a rough first run + the native-dep failure class; strongly rejected for crons — see Task 265, the scheduler flags make them unreliable by default, so defaulting a broken thing on is worse than off). **Prior art REQUIRED before deciding** (the D-375 rule): how do comparable local tools handle a heavy optional model download at install — prompt, lazy-on-first-use, or flag? That is reactive-by-accident: the feature author pays the tax, and a week with no feature PR would let a High-severity advisory sit unnoticed. **The gap:** we have Dependabot for direct deps but nothing that proactively catches a transitive advisory on a cadence, so they ambush whatever PR is open next. **Options:** (a) a scheduled workflow (weekly `npm audit fix --package-lock-only` → auto-PR, the lock-only pattern already proven twice this week — no `package.json` churn); (b) Dependabot grouped lockfile-only updates if it can be configured for transitives; (c) a `main`-branch `osv-scanner` cron that opens an issue rather than waiting for a PR to fail. Whichever: the fix must stay LOCK-ONLY when the range allows (both this week's fixes needed zero `package.json` change) and must not touch the published runtime surface silently. · Estimate: S · Depends: none · _**v0.6.6 lane (LANED 2026-08-05, D-425 — moved off a trigger: waiting for a 4th fire IS the tax this removes). - [x] 265. **The registered crons are killed the moment the user touches the keyboard and never retry — D-298's five-night starvation, now visible in the scheduler config (filed 2026-08-05; D-424).** **The evidence, read directly off the maintainer's machine** (`schtasks /query /xml` on `cmk-daily-distill`): `StopOnIdleEnd: true` + `RestartOnIdle: false` (killed on any input, never restarted), `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries: true` (never starts on battery), `StopIfGoingOnBatteries: true` (killed on unplug), and a 10-min idle REQUIREMENT before it may start (`IdleSettings.Duration PT10M`). **This is not a hypothesis — it is D-298's root cause made physical:** the 23:00 compression cron that was "killed mid-run five nights running with zero forward progress" was Windows killing it the moment the user returned to the machine, with `RestartOnIdle: false` guaranteeing no retry. A laptop on battery gets nothing, ever. **The scope beyond this one task:** every job `register-crons` schedules inherits these flags, so the whole Layer-6 maintenance tier (distill, curate) is unreliable by construction on the exact hardware most users have (a laptop). **The fix (Windows Task Scheduler leg of `register-crons.mjs`):** drop `StopOnIdleEnd`/the idle requirement (or set `RestartOnIdle: true`), and reconsider the battery blocks (a memory-compression pass is light — `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries` is inherited boilerplate, not a considered choice). **Cross-platform check REQUIRED:** verify the launchd (macOS) and cron/systemd (Linux) legs don't have an equivalent "give up if busy" posture — this is the platform-command-discipline class. **Compose with the resumability work:** even fixed, a scheduler is best-effort, so any load-bearing maintenance must ALSO be opportunistic + resumable (ADR-0020, and the Task 262 Q6/Q7 conclusion) — this task makes the cron less bad, it does not make it dependable. · Estimate: S–M · Depends: none · _**v0.6.6 lane (LANED 2026-08-05, D-425 — moved off a trigger: this is D-298's root cause with the evidence already in hand, not a speculative deferral). The scheduler-file change itself still needs the user's approval at PR time (U-7BWT4LXD).** Relates D-298 (the original starvation), ADR-0020 (resumable long jobs), Task 262 (which concluded crons cannot be load-bearing), D-424._

- [ ] 263. **Live-test parity — grow automated live coverage until the manual cut-gate finds nothing new (the user's standing directive, re-asserted 2026-08-04 after Task 261: "we need live tests for everything"; D-422).** **The measured gap:** **98 manual cut-gate stages** vs **6 automated live scripts, only 2 carrying real assertions** (health-whisper 13 checks, viewer 28). Everything else — install, capture, the whole recall ladder, cross-agent, health, crons, privacy — is a human walking a checklist once per release. **This is not a new rule** (the 2026-07-11 directive already binds: *"grow the AUTOMATED live coverage so the manual cut-gate finds less and less"*) — it is an admission we have been under-executing it, and Task 261 is the receipt. **The ordering principle, learned from 261 (the part that matters more than the count):** ☐ **(1) The AGED-CORPUS dimension first — it is a whole blind CLASS, not a missing test.** Every live script today does `mkdtemp` + `cmk install` and asserts on a fresh corpus; cut-gate W2 does the same. So anything that only manifests after N rebuilds/evictions/supersessions is invisible **by construction** — 261's vec-rowid desync is one instance, and consolidation, graduation, tombstone cascade and temporal sweep all live in the same shadow. The harness (build → mutate → rebuild → THEN assert, several cycles) **SHIPPED inside 261 (2026-08-04) as the REUSABLE seam this task adopts across the other scripts**: [`scripts/lib/aged-corpus.mjs`](../scripts/lib/aged-corpus.mjs) — `createAgedSandbox()` + `ageCorpus({cycles, afterCycle})` driving the REAL bins through write/expire/forget/supersede/unlink + `reindex --boot` + `reindex --full`, with the caller supplying assertions via `afterCycle` so nothing needs forking. Wired at 2 cycles into the always-on suite (`tests/cli-aged-corpus.test.js`) and 3 into the new `npm run live-verify:semantic` (which also closes the separate gap that semantic search had NO automated live coverage at all). Canonical HOW: design §17.14. **It earned its keep on run one**, surfacing two further bugs of the same family that no review had found — the `superseded_by` FK aborting `reindex --full` on any corpus with a supersession, and `meta.edges_built_at` outliving the dropped `edges` table (both fixed in 261; D-423). **Evidence that 'several cycles' is load-bearing:** against the pre-fix build the aged run survived cycle 1 and failed on cycle 2's full rebuild, while the FRESH baseline passed in the same run — a one-cycle harness reports green. ☐ **(2) Derived-state invariants as HEALTH CHECKS, not tests** — the thing that would have caught 261 cheaply was never a test: *every stored vector equals its own fact's cached vector*. That class (derived state agrees with source state) runs in production, on real aged corpora, without anyone remembering to run it. Wanted: index ↔ files, edges ↔ frontmatter, INDEX.md ↔ fact files, vec ↔ cache (261 ships this one). ☐ **(3) Port cut-gate stages by VALUE, not in order** — the 98 are not equal; automate first the ones that would hurt most if they broke silently (capture actually captures, recall actually recalls, install actually installs, privacy actually screens). ☐ **(4) Mark the genuinely-human stages as human** (does the status line LOOK right, does the viewer FEEL right) so the gate shrinks honestly instead of pretending. **Done when:** the tracked number moves — *cut-gate stages with an automated equivalent* — with a target of **12/98 → 60/98**, worked down every release rather than attempted in one push; the aged-corpus harness is adopted by every live-verify script where aging is meaningful; and the cut-gate guide marks each stage `[auto]` or `[human]` so the remaining manual surface is explicit. · Estimate: L (standing, per-release increments) · Depends: 261 (builds the harness) · _**Standing task, worked every release** (not a version lane — it is a ratchet). Relates the 2026-07-11 live-test directive, Task 261 (the receipt + the harness), D-422, the "unit-green ≠ works-on-real-input" rule and its newly-named sibling **fresh-green ≠ works-on-an-aged-corpus**._

- [ ] 262. **Write-time fact linking — populate `related:` automatically, so the memory has structure instead of 2,220 islands (grill-started 2026-08-04; BLOCKED on Task 261).** **The measurement that motivates it:** 2,220 facts, **89 linked (4.0%), 175 total edges** — and the breakdown indicts the writer, not the users: `project` facts (auto-extract, 97% of the corpus) are **3.7%** linked while hand-written types run 8.3% (`feedback`), 25% (`reference`), 33% (`user`). **Linking was always a human habit and we automated the writing without automating the habit.** The cause is mechanical: auto-extract sees only its own turn and never searches the corpus, so it cannot know the other end exists; `writeFact` has accepted a `related` option all along (`write-fact.mjs:218`) and nothing ever passes it. **Grill outcomes so far (Q1-Q8; Q5 answered by [outward research](../docs/research/2026-08-04-link-cap-practice.md)):** ☑ Q1 three similarity bands — duplicate/supersedes · related · nothing (reuses Task 143's already-calibrated near-dup seam rather than inventing one); ☑ Q2 backend-degrading — embeddings when semantic is installed, token-Jaccard otherwise, `similarity_backend` recorded per link exactly as the conflict queue already does; ☑ Q3 the "related" floor is **measured on the corpus, not picked** — and must be **computed at index time**, because the research showed 0.78 is only meaningful relative to THIS embedder's random-pair p99 (0.773); swap models and the constant is meaningless; ☑ Q4 write one direction, traverse both (the graph index already walks `in|out|both`, so backlinks are free without rewriting existing fact files); ☑ Q5 **cap 3 out-links, in-degree uncapped** — evidenced: our own UNCAPPED writer already chose median 2 / max 4; Memora caps 1-3; Wikipedia ships 3 in production; Beel's 3.4M-impression study inflects at 5-6 and 74% of digital libraries show 3-5; our own neighbour curve shows k=4 within 0.01 of k=12, i.e. past 3 you buy noise; ☑ Q6+Q7 **opportunistic, never scheduled** (see below); ☑ Q8 links auto-apply, merges keep going to the conflict queue (different risk, different treatment). **Q6/Q7 — the cron cannot be load-bearing, and we have the receipts:** the registered task on the maintainer's own machine carries `DisallowStartIfOnBatteries: true` (never starts on battery), `StopIfGoingOnBatteries: true` (killed on unplug), `StopOnIdleEnd: true` + `RestartOnIdle: false` (**killed the moment the user touches the keyboard, and never retried**) — which is **D-298's five-night starvation, visible in configuration**. `register-crons` is also a separate optional command `install` never calls, so most users have no cron at all. Therefore: bounded, resumable work attached to things that DO run (session start, reindex, explicit captures), cron as a pure accelerator; backfill of the existing 2,220 derives its resume point from which facts already carry links (ADR-0020, no sidecar watermark). **Still open:** the ADR-0023 posture — this resolves its DEFER by **firing its own named trigger** (extend the Task-99 benchmark with a relational qtype, baseline BEFORE linking, re-measure after) rather than revisiting it by argument; that benchmark work is sub-task 1 and the linking ships behind a flag so both measurements run on one build. **Sub-tasks:** ☑ **1 — the relational benchmark + its CANARY + the PRE-LINKING baseline** _(shipped 2026-08-08 — [`scripts/bench-linking.mjs`](../scripts/bench-linking.mjs) · `npm run bench:linking` · [`fixtures/link-bench/`](../fixtures/link-bench/) · [`tests/bench-linking.test.js`](../tests/bench-linking.test.js) · [baseline note](../docs/research/2026-08-08-linking-benchmark-baseline.md))._ **The trigger is MET, measured not argued:** flat hybrid answers **1.000** of the flat-answerable questions and **0.444** of the relational ones; hand-placed edges take the relational half to **0.889** with no flat-half loss. Canary margin **0.444** against a floor of 0.300 on a byte-identical unlinked twin, with three controls (single-hop / preference / temporal) proving the margin is attributable to `related:` links and not to traversal in general. **Two findings the linking design must answer:** (i) link traversal **DILUTES** — correct edges cost 0.25 on the temporal control by spending the traversal budget on a weak seed's neighbours, and it gets worse as coverage rises (zero when seeded from hybrid, so it is a seed-quality problem — seed relational expansion from hybrid, never the keyword ladder alone); (ii) one-shot keyword search answers **0 of 21** natural-language questions (implicit-AND, verified live on the real bin) — filed, NOT fixed, out of scope here. ☑ **2 — the linking mechanism, behind a flag** _(shipped 2026-08-08 — [`link-facts.mjs`](../packages/cli/src/link-facts.mjs) · the `writeFact` attachment · [`tests/cli-link-facts.test.js`](../tests/cli-link-facts.test.js), 22 tests)._ Three bands on every fact create: near-dup → a conflict-queue PROPOSAL (never a link, never a block — the fact is always captured); related → up to 3 out-links on the new fact's `related:`; below the floor → nothing. **The floor is derived at index time, never a constant** — the p99 of THIS corpus's own random-pair similarity under THIS backend, stored with provenance in the index `meta` table and recomputed on every full reindex (plus a boot-path re-derivation when the corpus drifts >50% from the sample it was built on). Two DERIVED degeneracy guards refuse to link at all when the distribution cannot separate signal from noise (floor ≥ the calibrated near-dup ceiling, or floor ≤ the random-pair median) — the D-433 dilution risk answered at its source. Backend-degrades: token-Jaccard always available, semantic cosine via an injected prepared backend (`linkSimilarity`), `backend` recorded per link. Flag: `CMK_LINK_FACTS` > `memory.link_facts` > **default OFF** (D-436 — see sub-task 4). ☑ **3 — the backfill** _(shipped 2026-08-08 — [`link-backfill.mjs`](../packages/cli/src/link-backfill.mjs) · `cmk autolink [--dry-run] [--max n] [--tier T] [--semantic]` · [`tests/cli-link-backfill.test.js`](../tests/cli-link-backfill.test.js), 8 tests)._ ADR-0020 shape: the smallest durable unit is ONE fact, each persists before the next is considered, and the resume point is derived from TWO ARTIFACTS — the fact's own `related:` frontmatter (linked) and a `link_eval` row keyed by id + content-sha in the REBUILDABLE index (considered, nothing above the floor). No watermark sidecar: losing `link_eval` to a full reindex costs a re-consideration, which is idempotent work, never lost work. Bounded (250/run) with `remaining` re-derived from the same artifacts the next run reads. `--dry-run` writes nothing at all — not the files, not the markers, not the audit. Never queues near-dups (both facts already exist and were both accepted); never touches a fact that already carries links. ☑ **4 — the AFTER re-measurement** _(shipped 2026-08-08 — `npm run bench:linking -- --after [--semantic]`, two automatic variants added beside the BEFORE and the ceiling; [research note §7](../docs/research/2026-08-08-linking-benchmark-baseline.md))._ **THE RESULT IS NEGATIVE AND IT IS THE FINDING (D-436, restated after review finding B1):** read the two automatic variants SEPARATELY. On `graph-hybrid` multi-hop R@5 (fresh, and aged agrees): unlinked **0.444** · `auto-write` **0.444** (no change — and **unmeasured**: 4 edges over 3 of 50 facts, because the write path links backward only and no floor exists below 24 facts) · `auto-backfill` **0.333** · hand-placed **0.889**. The BACKFILL is the fair test (comparable density: 19 edges/14 facts vs 13/11) and it **recovers 0% of the available gain** while the temporal control loses **0.25** — **D-433's condition fires on it, the controls are the judge.** The earlier "−0.111 regression" claim is WITHDRAWN: one multi-hop question is worth 0.111, the instrument's resolution floor, which this project's own canary rationale calls single-question wobble. Not a tuning knob: the fixture's multi-hop edges join facts that are topically related and lexically far apart, which is the axis a similarity-ranked linker is weakest on, so a lower floor buys more of the edges that already made recall worse. The measurement points back at ADR-0023's own first-ranked deferred candidate (LLM-derived `cues:`). It also FOUND TWO REAL DEFECTS no unit test could see (D-435). **RATIFIED (D-436), re-adjudicated after B1: the write path ships DEFAULT-OFF on ONE ground** — an unbudgeted **+2.8 s per capture** on the real 2,260-fact corpus (11.6 s → 14.4 s, real bin) that grows linearly and lands on the same synchronous path the detached auto-extract child runs under the §8.5 hook ceiling (composition unverified). The recall regression is the BACKFILL's and is no longer cited as a reason to default the WRITE PATH off — it is why ADR-0023's deferred LLM candidate is still the live one. The mechanism, flag, derived floor and backfill all ship COMPLETE; `cmk autolink` (dry-run default, `--apply` to write) is the no-configuration opt-in, and `memory.link_facts: true` turns the write path on. The negative result STANDS as the finding — the task ships with the honest posture rather than a default that quietly makes recall worse. · Estimate: M (benchmark S + linking M + backfill S) · **Depends: Task 261 — SATISFIED, 261 shipped 2026-08-04, so this is UNBLOCKED** (the index it would have written into is now correct; the block was real — auto-linking on a corrupt index would have written ~4,000 wrong links into the tier whose entire value is trustworthiness) · _**v0.6.6 lane — the HEADLINE (with Task 268 as the visual half).** Relates ADR-0023 (DEFER resolved by trigger, not argument), Task 143 (the calibrated near-dup seam this reuses), Task 25 (the conflict queue it routes duplicates to), D-400 ("an EDGE-AUTHORING problem"), D-421, the [link-cap research](../docs/research/2026-08-04-link-cap-practice.md), the [2026-08-07 research break](../docs/research/2026-08-07-research-break-two-papers-two-repos.md) (D-428 — MRAgent makes the edge layer load-bearing; Zero-Mem is benchmark evidence FOR the deterministic path and adds entity/term co-occurrence as a third edge source; mnemory's render-time co-occurrence edge LAYER is a cheap viewer rider; LoCoMo/LongMemEval's single-hop/multi-hop/temporal/preference taxonomy fits sub-task 1's benchmark; KiroCrew auto_improvement's CANARY discipline binds sub-task 1 — before measuring linking's effect, prove the benchmark detects a KNOWN win, e.g. a hand-linked subset must beat its unlinked twin, else the A/B measures noise)._

- [ ] 260. **The viewer's visual pass — `cmk view` works and is ugly; make it look designed (the user's verdict on first sight, 2026-08-03: "even just from looking at the first page i can say it is ugly").** **The gap this closes is in the PROCESS, not just the CSS:** the 255 grill asked what the viewer should SHOW and never what it should LOOK LIKE, so "legibility over polish" was a lead's passing call rather than a ratified criterion, and visual quality was never a done-condition. Recorded so the next UI-bearing grill asks the question. **Constraint UNCHANGED (design §24.1.7):** one static HTML file, inline vanilla CSS/JS, zero dependencies, fully offline. The [research](../docs/research/2026-08-03-viewer-visual-design.md) settles that the constraint was never the problem — **Deno's docs ship 117 KB of CSS with zero drop-shadows; Datasette has one; both look good.** **The eight changes, ranked by visual impact per line of CSS:** ☑ (1) give the fact card a **title** + clamp the measure to ~760px (biggest single win — every card is currently one undifferentiated 15px paragraph at ~1040px, which is the wall-of-text effect; claude-mem's feed column is 650px and stacks four typographic tiers); ☑ (2) **tint** the badges instead of outlining them (one rule: `bg rgba(hue,.14)` / `border rgba(hue,.30)` / text in the hue — five outlined pills per card is visual static); ☑ (3) real **surface separation** — the page is currently `#fbfaf8` on `#ffffff` panels, a 2% delta, so borders do 100% of the work and nothing reads as a surface; adopt a warm ladder (claude-mem's `#2b2520` ink / `#efebe4` panel / `#1a1916` dark ground — warm neutrals, never browser grey); ☑ (4) **four type sizes** + one uppercase micro-label (`11px/600/uppercase/.12em`); ☑ (5) fix the **rhythm** — card padding is 13px while card gap is 9px, so cards crowd each other more than their own contents; ☑ (6) **sticky header** with blur + real pill tabs (the active tab currently uses a `border-bottom: none` seam trick and reads as a seam); ☑ (7) shrink the **health strip** to an inline pill — a full-width pale-green bar for the GOOD state is the loudest element on the page; it becomes a bar only when not-ok; ☑ (8) **rescue the graph** — drop degree-0 nodes (a visible picture-frame of ~150 orphan dots), label only degree≥3 + hovered, tint the canvas, `paint-order: stroke` halos so labels survive overlap, supersession edges at higher opacity than the rest. **Runners-up (3 lines each):** ☑ snippets leak raw markdown (`**Global install**:` renders literally — visible in the user's own screenshot); ☑ the tier `<select>` is 335px wide because its option labels are prose (move the explanation to `title`); ☑ the health table's CHECK column wraps while 200px sits empty (switch to a row layout: glyph · title · right-aligned status · detail · fix command). **Done when:** the eight land, `npm test` + stress green, live-verify still 25/25 (the visual pass must not touch behavior — the read-only matrix, the Host guard and the strip semantics are all pinned), and **the user looks at it in a real browser and says it's better** — the one criterion no test can assert, and the reason this task exists. **Follow-ups filed by the 260 review (NOT this pass — each needs work the visual pass was forbidden or is a feature in its own right):** ☐ (a) **flattened bullet runs reach TITLES, not just snippets** — measured over 200 facts, 48 snippets AND 15 titles carry a `- A - B - C` run, plus 8 titles under 20 chars that orphan their value into the body; the cause is the SERVER's `flatten(rich.headline, 240)`, so fixing it is a payload change (Trigger: the next task that touches the facts payload shape); ☐ (b) **collision-aware graph label placement** — truncation + halos reduced the overlap but two hubs parked close still collide; a real fix is a placement pass, i.e. a feature (Trigger: a user reports the graph unreadable, or the graph gains a second consumer); ☐ (c) **the title splitter is polynomial** — 520 ms at 32 kB, harmless today because every input is local and server-capped (Trigger: if `headline`/`why`/`how` ever gain an untrusted-length path, e.g. a team-tier or remote payload). · Estimate: M · Depends: 255 shipped · _**v0.6.5 lane** (patch = polish on a shipped feature, per D-24's one-differentiator-per-minor rule; v0.7.0 stays reserved for the team layer). - [x] 258. _closed 2026-08-02 — **REJECTED with evidence** (D-416); no PR. The trigger fired (250 shipped), the precondition ran, and the [noise-floor measurement](../docs/research/2026-08-02-stale-refs-noise-floor.md) killed it: **49.1% of path-shaped refs don't resolve naively, 39.1% with the skip-list**, and hand-classifying ALL 43 surviving tokens found ~20 slash-as-"or" prose (`README/CLI.md/MCP.md`), ~12 other-repos' files from research notes (`letta/constants.py`), ~8 tier-relative shorthand (`archive/evicted-bullets.md`) and exactly **2 genuinely-gone files — both correct to be gone** (Task 186's validator consolidation). 2 benign hits per 151 checked at a 39% false-positive rate fails Task 250's own actionability bar (D-412) by an order of magnitude; wiring it would degrade the whisper channel we just built. **Named re-open condition:** facts capturing references in STRUCTURED frontmatter (`refs:`) at write time rather than prose — a schema change, not a scanner change. The awrshift borrow was right for THEIR corpus (dated prose bullets, few cross-references); ours is reference-dense in a way that inverts the economics._ **`stale-refs` scan — a deterministic does-this-fact-still-describe-the-world check (the one borrow from the awrshift name-twin dive where a peer in our shape-class is genuinely ahead; filed 2026-07-29, D-410; MIT — techniques with attribution).** **The gap:** the kit has NO structural check that a stored fact's file-path references still resolve on disk — a fact citing `src/foo.mjs` stays confidently recallable years after the file is renamed. Their `stale-refs.py` (130 lines): an advisory-only scan of the always-injected layer for path references that no longer resolve, wired into session-start; the non-obvious value is the skip-list + multi-root resolver (a naive version drowns in false positives). **The build (S–M):** ☐ a deterministic scan over the snapshot-injected tiers (MEMORY.md bullets + high-trust facts) extracting path-shaped references and stat-ing them; ☐ MEASURE THE NOISE FLOOR FIRST on the real corpus (our docs are heavy with §-refs/ADR ids/code fences — the D-410 caveat: any wiring waits on a measured false-positive rate); ☐ advisory surface only (a Task-250-whisper input or a `cmk stats` line — never a blocker); ☐ skip-list + multi-root resolution adapted to our tier layout. · Estimate: S (scan) + S (noise measurement) · Depends: none · _**Trigger (D-248): rides Task 250's build (a whisper input) OR the next `cmk stats` touch, whichever first.** Relates the [awrshift dive](../docs/research/2026-07-29-awrshift-claude-memory-kit-dive.md), Task 250 (the surface), D-410._

Live-captured from a real `claude -p` run through a real SessionStart hook, both halves: `{"session_id":"c6777e8b-…","cwd":"C:\\tmp\\cmk253\\payload","hook_event_name":"SessionStart","source":"startup"}` and, on `--resume` of the same id, the identical shape with `"source":"resume"`. **Pointer mode fires on `resume` ONLY, not `resume`+`compact`:** Anthropic's own docs describe compaction as summarizing older history to free space, so the pre-compaction snapshot survives as a SUMMARY, not verbatim — pointing at content that may have been summarized away could silently cost a session its memory. `compact` therefore keeps the full snapshot (it is exactly when re-anchoring is worth its bytes); `fork` / unknown / absent / unparseable all fail open to full.
  - **Static/volatile split chosen (conservative — "when in doubt, keep injecting"):** REPLACED = the tier blocks + the state-label instruction that only describes labels inside them. STILL INJECTED = the authoritative-memory preamble (D-40 under-fire class), the existence advertisement (§20.8 — worth MORE when the bulk is absent), the temporal mention and the commit proposal (genuinely volatile; a resumed session has not seen the git dirtiness that accrued). Empty-memory projects emit no pointer at all.
  - **Review finding #1 (RATIFIED) — the pointer declines itself when it would not save.** Reproduced independently on the real bin: a one-bullet project emits **673 B on startup but 778 B on resume** (+105 B), because the preserved preamble + advertisement + pointer line outweigh a two-line body — the premise inverts for exactly the fresh-install shape. The pointer is now taken only when `pointerBytes < fullBytes` **and** `≤ capBytes`; when either guard rejects, the FULL snapshot is emitted **and labelled `full` everywhere** (return value, status line, `recall.log`), which also closes the mislabel where a cap-rejected full emission still logged `mode: pointer` and corrupted the pointer-vs-full aggregation §20.9 rests on. Same fix for the empty-memory case. Verified live after the fix: tiny corpus resume 673 B `mode=full`; the rich sandbox still saves 189 B (1163 → 974) `mode=pointer`.

Bounded by the frozen-snapshot contract (writes land next session start), by most capture going to the search-reachable archive rather than the snapshot, and by the existence advertisement — still injected in pointer mode — whose fact count + latest-capture date DO move. **Trigger to close:** gate pointer mode on tier mtime vs. the session's last `inject` entry ts (both artifacts already exist; no new sidecar state).

Door 5: `bytes` + `mode` on the `recall.log` inject entry.

No child reads cwd; every argv path is absolute. Each shape now lives in ONE exported descriptor (`autoExtractSpawnDescriptor` / `lazyCompressSpawnDescriptor` / `precompactWorkerSpawnDescriptor`) so it is Door-3 assertable without a real spawn — and so the precompact twins stop carrying two copies of the same options object. **Guard:** tmpdir is pinned only when a non-empty `projectRoot` is present, because each child's last-resort fallback is `process.cwd()` and re-pointing that at tmpdir without handing over a root would scaffold a memory tree in the temp dir.

- [x] 250. _shipped 2026-08-02, PR #334._ **Failure-driven health nudge + a troubleshooting skill — make the kit tell the AI when something's broken and how to fix it (the user's design, 2026-07-22).** **The gap (`U-U5PPSG7Y`, high trust):** users won't run `cmk doctor`; silent failures must surface automatically. The kit already writes its own failure signals (`extract.log`, spawn-error entries, `audit.log`, `poison-guard.log`); the nudge fires ONLY when a FRESH failure exists there — fix it → next run succeeds → no fresh signal → no nudge. **Build (two halves):** ☐ extend `buildMemoryHint` (capture-prompt.mjs — the existing per-prompt hint) with a CHEAP fresh-failure check (log tail / marker file, NOT a doctor run — hot path); whisper '⚠ a kit op failed — the troubleshooting skill can diagnose'; ☐ a NEW troubleshooting skill (scaffolded like memory-write/search) that teaches `cmk doctor` + repair/reindex/forget on the whisper or a recognized symptom, extensible to other cmk verbs. **Prior art: NONE** — D-374 corpus gap 'self-healing CLI repair UX'; **outward research + a grill REQUIRED before building** (D-375). **→ PRIOR-ART UPDATE 2026-07-22 (D-393): the gap PARTIALLY closed** — the [Octopoda-OS code dive](../docs/research/2026-07-22-octopoda-os-code-dive.md) supplies real, code-verified detection prior art: 10 deterministic loop classifiers (pure functions over an event log — the shape 212/250 want), and most on-point, reflection.py's **pattern-aware false-positive suppressor** (recognizes known-benign key shapes — rolling summaries, heartbeats, recovery markers — and suppresses the alert), which is a shipped precedent for THIS task's noise-threshold amendment. Still missing from the corpus: the self-heal-first repair UX half (Octopoda detects + pauses; it does not repair) — the grill still owes outward research there. **TWO AMENDMENTS from the 2026-07-22 review (bake into the grill's frame):** (1) **self-heal-first scoping (U-LNFPaXTT: self-heal first, report last)** — as first filed this task was ALL report, contradicting the kit's own recorded lesson AND the user's 248 principle ('doctor should only be part of the fix, not the actual fix'). The hierarchy: failure classes the kit can safely fix itself get FIXED automatically (the 248 model); the whisper covers ONLY what genuinely needs judgment (missing agent CLI, broken hook registration, a reinstall decision). (2) **noise threshold** — a single transient failure (one Haiku timeout that the next run recovers from) is non-actionable noise; the whisper must key on ACTIONABLE failure (repeated, or failed-without-subsequent-success), never raw fresh-failure-exists.   - **GRILLED 2026-07-29 — design RATIFIED (D-412); outward research done ([self-healing-CLI-repair-UX note](../docs/research/2026-07-29-self-healing-cli-repair-ux.md) — the D-374 gap's other half paid; honest gap confirmed: the whisper composition is novel, the ingredients all have shipped precedent). The 8 settled points:**
    1. **Split rule:** auto-fix ONLY kit-owned + reversible + idempotent, piggybacked on ops already running, self-verified by the next run succeeding; everything else whispers; **unknown failure classes default to whisper, never auto-fix** (the industry-convergent line — brew/git-maintenance/rustup silent class + the Nx deterministic-and-verified gate).
    2. **Actionability (noise threshold):** deterministic failures (can't transiently recover: agent CLI missing, hooks unregistered/drifted, corrupt install) whisper on FIRST strike; stochastic failures (spawn timeouts, API/network errors) need **≥2 consecutive same-class failures with no success between** — a success resets the streak. Freshness guard on both: evidence ≤7 days old.
    3. **Cadence:** whisper present on EVERY prompt while actionable, STATELESS (no did-I-whisper sidecar — self-clean is structural, the Tailscale present-only-when-broken model; their stuck-warning bug #19241 is the trap statefulness invites). One line max; multiple active classes → highest severity + count; `dependsOn` cascade (upstream failure suppresses downstream whispers).
    4. **Evidence store:** ONE append-only `health.log` (NDJSON `{class, outcome: ok|fail, ts, detail}`, audit-log discipline); instrumented ops append BOTH outcomes; hot path tail-reads a bounded chunk and computes per-class streaks. **Event-driven only — no environment probing on the hot path**; deterministic failures enter when a real op hits them (spawn ENOENT → `agent-cli-missing`).
    5. **Troubleshooting skill = per-code repair book:** whisper carries stable code + one-liner + skill pointer; the skill holds a keyed section per code (symptom → diagnosis → exact fix → class: silent-safe / confirm-first / advise-only), with the confirm-first discipline written in (ADR-0018: fixes touching user-owned state are PROPOSED for one-tap approval, never just run — the Claude-Code-`/doctor` precedent). Kit failure codes only; doctor is the fallback section.
    6. **Channels:** whisper = `additionalContext`, model-only. A visible `systemMessage` line ONLY at severity `memory-off` (kit effectively not capturing — silence there loses sessions unknowingly).
    7. **Wave-1 instrumentation:** capture chain (capture-turn → auto-extract spawn + outcome), inject-context + lazy-compress spawn, precompact worker, reindex/index-drift, MCP tool errors. Cron-side jobs later via the same append. **Recorded limit:** a fully dead hook layer kills the whisper too (event-driven = a hook that never fires writes nothing); total hook death stays covered by install auto-recover (248) / doctor / MCP surfaces. Partial hook failure — the common case — is covered.
    8. **Registry** is Warnable-shaped (`code, title, severity, dependsOn, primaryAction, class, strikeThreshold`) and is the extension point — **Task 258 stays SEPARATE**, entering later as a low-severity advisory class once its noise floor is measured.
  - **Done when:** ☑ `health.log` module + registry with the 8-point semantics, unit-tested at the streak/freshness/cascade edges (budget-pair discipline); ☑ wave-1 sites append both outcomes (Door 5 assertions); ☑ `buildMemoryHint` emits the whisper (fail-open, privacy-screened, byte-bounded) — **automatic-path criterion (D-169): a seeded failure streak makes the whisper appear in the REAL hook bin's output with NO manual command run** (live-verify script, real bin, sandboxed tiers); ☑ `memory-off` severity emits the systemMessage line, live-verified; ☑ troubleshooting skill scaffolded + installed + `KIT_ALLOW` entry (validate-skill-allowlist both directions); ☑ a doctor HC reads `health.log` and reports active warnings; ☑ docs walk: design.md § (canonical mechanism), README capability line, CHANGELOG, glossary (whisper / health log / failure code), CLI.md–MCP.md N/A unless a verb/tool changes, research note + SOURCES (done at grill).
  - **BUILT 2026-08-01 — all 8 ratified points implemented; canonical HOW is [design §23](design.md).** No point was deviated from. **Six judgement calls inside the ratified frame, each pinned by a test, flagged for the lead's ratification:**
    1. **Wave-1 code names + one severity choice.** `agent-cli-missing` · `extract-failing` · `inject-failing` · `precompact-failing` · `index-drift` · `mcp-tool-failing`. `index-drift` is the only one graded **advisory** rather than degraded (a stale INDEX under-fires the recall hint; nothing is lost) and the only one with `fixClass: silent` — so all three severities and all three fix classes have a live member rather than being enum decoration.
    2. **`capture-turn` + the lazy-compress spawn are ONE-SIDED (fail only).** Spawning is not extracting; an `ok` there would clear a real extraction-failure streak on every later turn. The detached child owns its own `ok`.
    3. **`inject-context` records ONE verdict per RUN**, the worst thing in it. The obvious alternative (`ok` for the snapshot + `fail` for the spawn) writes `[ok, fail]` on EVERY session start, pinning the tail streak at 1 forever — a two-strike class could never fire, and the signal would be **dead while looking instrumented**. This one was nearly shipped the wrong way; it is the composition worth re-reading at review.
    4. **MCP: a THROWN handler is a failure, an `isError` envelope is not.** `isError` is a tool reporting an ordinary outcome (id not found, guard rejection) — whispering on it would nag a user who asked for something absent.
    5. **HC-14 SKIPs (not PASSes) when the log is unreadable**, deliberately diverging from the reader's fail-open contract: fail-open is right on the hot path and a false green in a diagnostic. Needs BOTH `statSync().isFile()` and `openSync` — POSIX rejects a directory with EISDIR, Windows opens it happily.
    6. **A latent bug fixed en route:** `capture-turn`'s detached spawn had no `'error'` listener, so an async ENOENT became an **uncaught exception in the Stop hook**. Now caught and recorded, with the honest bound in-comment (the child is unref'd and the hook exits fast, so the event usually never arrives — it is a crash guard, not a reliable detector).
  - **The self-review round found four more things, all fixed rather than reported:** (a) **a point-5 compliance gap** — the whisper carried the title but NOT the stable `code` the repair book is sectioned by, which the skill's own text already assumed was there; the code now leads the line and survives the byte clip. (b) **the byte cap was escapable** — title-only clipping cannot honor the budget when the fixed part alone exceeds it (unreachable with the frozen registry, but a future long `primaryAction` would have silently blown a budget `validate-budget-pairs` claims is enforced); a final unconditional clamp closes it. (c) **the one PURE function could THROW** on an unparseable `now` (`new Date(x).toISOString()` raises RangeError) — in a module whose whole contract is fail-open. (d) **`buildMcpServer`'s JSDoc had been orphaned** onto the new wrapper.
  - **REVIEW ROUND (the independent pass — 3 Blocking + 2 Important + 3 Minor, all reproduced, all fixed on-branch).** The three Blocking findings were all the same shape: *a claim in the design that the code did not honor.*
    1. **B1 — the prescribed fix could not clear the warning it prescribed.** `index-drift` says ``fix: `cmk reindex` ``, but `reindex.mjs` appended nothing; the only writer of the clearing `ok` was `writeFact`'s inline rebuild. An agent doing exactly as told watched the warning persist for up to the 7-day window — **the stuck-warning class §23.1 calls structurally impossible**, with the added insult that the kit prescribed the one command that could not help. The clear moved into `reindex()` itself, so every rebuild route resolves it. A second instance surfaced while wiring it: `writeFact`'s `fail` had to move to the transition form too, or a stale in-process `ok` would suppress the real clearing one. **Generalized into design §23.4:** every registry entry's `primaryAction` must be a command that actually writes that class's `ok`; a test pins the pairing.
    2. **B2 — a sparse class was structurally UNREACHABLE, not merely delayed.** The bounded tail is shared across all classes, so per-tool-call and per-write `ok`s evicted a genuine two-strike streak before the whisper read it. Fixed at the source with `appendHealthTransition` (per-process, per-class memory; `ok` written only on a transition, `fail` never suppressed) at the two high-cadence sites, plus `HEALTH_TAIL_BYTES` 16 KB → 64 KB as defence-in-depth. It preserves the streak semantics exactly — the `ok` between two fails always lands, so the clearing transition is never the one dropped. The composition test the review named as missing now exists (a sparse streak survives 400 tool calls + 200 fact writes; a second case proves the raised budget alone absorbs 600 plain appends). **This also resolved M3** — a healthy class now writes one line per process instead of one per operation.
    3. **B3 — the skill's authority exceeded the boundary the design claimed.** `Bash(cmk repair *)` admitted `--hooks` (rewrites `settings.json`) and `--format` (migrates committed memory), while §23.5 asserted both were absent — and the "cannot mutate memory" test passed anyway, having only looked for `cmk remember`-shaped verbs. **Deviation from the arbitration, taken under its own escape clause:** repair is ungranted ENTIRELY rather than narrowed to `--index`, because a `Bash()` pattern is a PREFIX match — `Bash(cmk repair --index*)` also admits `cmk repair --index --hooks`, and a grant with a hole is worse than no grant. `Bash(cmk stats *)` dropped as unused. Grants now pinned both ways (exact positive list + a blocklist).
    - **I1** HC-14's SKIP-on-unreadable documented in `HEALTH-CHECKS.md` (table row + self-repair section), matching how HC-12/HC-5 document theirs. **I2** `detail` is now structurally enforced (`DETAIL_TOKEN_PATTERN`; a violating value becomes `invalid-detail`, never written through, never thrown on) — the prose said "never user content" and nothing checked it, so the first site to pass `err.message` would have put stderr into a file the whisper reads. **M1** repaired collateral damage in Task 177's entry (a pre-existing embedded CR that this task's diff turned into a real newline, splitting the entry). **M2** the no-scaffold guard now requires the install marker `context/MEMORY.md` rather than a bare `context/` directory, so an unrelated repo with a `context/` folder never gets a health log.
    - **Follow-up recorded, deliberately NOT done this round:** extend `validate-skill-sources` to check each skill's grants against a per-skill allowlist, so a widened grant fails the suite rather than relying on per-skill tests. **Trigger (D-248): the next skill-surface task.**
  - **A scope bound recorded rather than assumed (design §23.3, with a named trigger):** the whisper is **Claude-Code-only** in wave 1. The Kiro/Cursor/Codex dispatchers call `capturePrompt` but have never emitted the per-prompt recall hint either, so those legs have no prompt-hook output surface to carry it — a PRE-EXISTING asymmetry the whisper inherits, not a regression. The consequence worth naming: the troubleshooting skill DOES ship to Kiro, so those users have the repair book and reach it through **HC-14** instead of a whisper. Also recorded there: the log's growth posture (the MCP `ok` append is its busiest writer; rotation trigger at ~5 MB alongside `audit.log`) and its concurrent-append safety.
  - **Gates (FINAL code, post-review-round — re-run in full 2026-08-02 after a machine crash killed the first verification pass mid-stress):** full suite **3849 passed / 210 files**, every validator green; **`npm run stress` 5/5 on the first invocation** (1375.6s total; the gate binds — this task touches hook handlers and spawn sites); 5 test files added (107 tests at first build, grown by the review round) + 6 touched. **Live-verified** by a new [`scripts/live-verify-health-whisper.mjs`](../scripts/live-verify-health-whisper.mjs) (`npm run live-verify:health-whisper`) — real `cmk install` into a throwaway project with an isolated `MEMORY_KIT_USER_DIR`, a failure streak seeded into `health.log`, then the REAL `cmk-capture-prompt` bin driven with a realistic stdin payload and **no manual command anywhere**: **11/11** checks pass (whisper present · carries the stable code · names the fix · fires on a 2-char prompt where the recall hint is null · `memory-off` systemMessage as a sibling field · hook still exits 0 · single blip stays silent · self-clean after one success · **B1 regression: the prescribed `cmk reindex` actually CLEARS the warning** · HC-14 and the whisper agree on the same evidence). Zero LLM, zero network, so it is cheap to re-run unlike its live-verify siblings.

- [ ] 244. **Pack-trust assessor — let recall say "I'm not confident enough to answer from this" (the ONE genuinely new idea from the 2026-07-21 research pass; D-380).** **What the kit does today:** recall returns the best-scoring facts it has. Trust exists per-FACT (`trust_score`, the 151.T/194 lineage; the search rank blends it once a fact has ≥3 outcome signals) — but there is **no assessment of the PACK as a whole**, and no path for the agent to abstain or escalate. A query whose best hits are three medium-trust, mutually-unrelated, six-month-old facts returns them with exactly the same confidence posture as one whose top hit is a high-trust, corroborated, recent fact. The agent has no signal that the second answer is worth more than the first.
  **The borrow (Mnemo, hackathon MemoryAgent — [note](../docs/research/2026-07-21-mnemo-qwen-memoryagent.md)):** a runtime **assessor** scores the retrieved pack before the agent answers from it, and can route to abstain / escalate / answer-with-caveat. Their pack-trust rule is a MINIMUM (the pack is only as trustworthy as its weakest load-bearing member), not an average — an average lets one strong fact launder three weak ones. **This has no analog anywhere in the kit or the backlog** (verified against Tasks 194/151.T/212/233 and design §9.4 — all per-fact or per-query-rank, none pack-level).
  **The second, harder half (also Mnemo's, and the reason their v3→v4 fix was a SCORING bug not a model bug): the metric must be two-sided.** Unsafe-acceptance alone is gameable — a system that abstains constantly scores perfectly on it. Any gate here ships with its **over-abstention counter-metric**, and both semantics are written down BEFORE the first number is computed. This is the same discipline D-124/§17.10 applies to budgets (test the edges, not the middle), applied to a judgment gate.
  **Open design questions (grill before building — this is deliberately NOT specced yet):** ☐ where does it run — inside `cmk search`'s result assembly, in the memory-search skill's ladder, or as an MCP-tool response field the model reads? ☐ is the verdict a number, a band (high/medium/low), or a sentence the agent reads? ☐ does it gate anything, or is it purely advisory (the kit's fail-open posture and the ADR-0017 "judgments never auto-rank" line both push hard toward ADVISORY)? ☐ what feeds it — trust_score minimum, recency spread, corroboration count, score gap between hit 1 and hit 5, or the recall-log's own miss history? ☐ how does it compose with Task 233's gated pointer-hint (that decides WHETHER to hint; this decides HOW MUCH to trust what came back — adjacent, not the same gate).
  **Done when:** ☐ the assessor is a PURE function over a result set (testable without a corpus, per the boundary-test discipline); ☐ both metrics defined in writing before implementation, with the over-abstention counter-metric asserted by a test that FAILS a constantly-abstaining assessor; ☐ advisory-only unless a grill session decides otherwise (never silently suppresses a result); ☐ a real-corpus check on this repo's ~1,880 facts showing the score separates a known-strong query from a known-weak one. · Estimate: M (S to implement, M because the metric design is the actual work) · Depends: none (composes with 233 if that lands first) · _**NOT LANED — needs a grill session first** (the design questions above are genuine forks, not implementation details; laning it before that would be the fabricated-commitment shape D-267 warns about). **Trigger (D-248):** lane it when EITHER (a) the recall-surface lane (232/233) ships and the ladder is stable enough to hang a confidence signal off, OR (b) a live session shows the agent answering confidently from a demonstrably weak pack (the failure this prevents — file it the moment it's observed). Relates D-380, [the Mnemo note](../docs/research/2026-07-21-mnemo-qwen-memoryagent.md), Task 194 (per-fact outcome signals — the input this aggregates), Task 212 (the stats surface where its metrics would live), ADR-0017 (judgments never auto-rank — the constraint on making it a gate)._

- [x] 252. _shipped 2026-07-28, PR #330._ **`maskPii` email-pattern hardening — dotted local-parts slip the screen (found by Task 233's review round, 2026-07-22; PRE-EXISTING kit-wide gap, not a 233 regression).** **The gap:** `maskPii` masks `bob@corp.io` → `«EMAIL»` but does NOT match dotted local-parts (`alice.leak@x.com`) — every disk write that relies on the L1 screen (transcript capture, hint telemetry, any future prompt-derived write) inherits it. Found when a review-round PII test passed for the wrong reason (tokenization split the dots) and was strengthened honestly. **The build (S):** ☑ audit `pii-patterns`/`maskPii` against a real-world email corpus shape (dots, plus-addressing, subdomains) + the other pattern classes for sibling gaps; ☑ fix the regex(es); ☑ regression tests per shape incl. the exact `alice.leak@x.com` case; ☑ verify no over-match (an `@` in code/log text that is not an email).

  **PREMISE FALSIFIED, and the audit found the real gaps instead (2026-07-28).** The stated gap — dotted local-parts slip the screen — **does not reproduce**: `EMAIL_RE`'s local-part class has carried `.` since Task 148 (`git log -S 'EMAIL_RE'` = one commit, 7fe820d), `maskPii('alice.leak@x.com')` → `«EMAIL»` on the pre-fix module, and `tests/cli-screen-then-mask.test.js:155` had been pinning a DOTTED address (`someone.real@gmail.com`) masking end-to-end through the real `cmk remember` the whole time. D-395's by-product note reasoned from a test that passed for the wrong reason to a conclusion about the regex, without running the regex — the falsifying check was one `node -e`. The original claim is preserved above rather than rewritten (decision-trail rule); what the shape audit actually found, and this task shipped, is: **under-match (leaks)** — non-ASCII local parts/domains masked only from the first ASCII byte (`añez@x.com` → `añ«EMAIL»`, `a@münchen.de` unmatched: the ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9]` class + an ASCII-only `\b`), **in BOTH normalization forms** — the review round reproduced the same half-mask on DECOMPOSED (NFD) input after the first fix, because `\p{L}` does not match a combining mark and macOS `ls`/`git` output is NFD, so the classes and both lookarounds carry `\p{M}`; the `nnn.nnn.nnnn` and `+CC-NN-NNNNNNN` phone forms; and a case-SENSITIVE `USERNAME` pattern (`Some.User` shipped while `some.user` masked). **Over-match (corruption)** — `git@github.com:owner/repo.git` and `logo@2x.png` both destroyed as `«EMAIL»`; the fix's own first cut then over-corrected, `$`-anchoring the file-extension exemption so a REAL address wearing an extension (`exports/john.doe@corp.com.csv`) masked on main and leaked on the branch — the exemption now strips the extension and re-tests the stem against a fully-anchored address. **A second-order gate bug** — the cheap keyword pre-filter (`mightContainPii`) is a GATE, not a hint, and silently disabled the new dotted-phone pattern (and the widened case-insensitive username) for text carrying none of its trigger characters; patterns now live in ONE registry whose bare per-pattern samples the suite loops, so a gate-dead pattern fails the suite instead of shipping as a no-op. Named residuals, tested not assumed: RFC-quoted local parts, bare E.164, and two shape-identical phone false positives (`100.200.3000`, `+NNN NNN NNNNNNN`) accepted in the masking direction. SSH-remote ruling: `git@`/`hg@` allowlisted (one string for every user on earth), a personal login in a remote still masks.

  **Detour taken (D-406's named trigger fired):** this task's stress gate binds (the module is imported by the `capture-turn`/`capture-prompt` hook path) and went **4/5** on `cli-backfill.test.js` — the same setup-phase class D-406 recorded as environment data with the trigger *"if this setup-phase class hits a PR where the gate DOES bind, root-causing it becomes a blocking detour."* Root-caused rather than re-waved: **it is a HOOK TIMEOUT.** `vitest.config.js` deliberately raised `testTimeout` to 30 s for vitest 4's subprocess/IO overhead but left `hookTimeout` at vitest's 10 s default; the failing test measured **11 283 ms** while every sibling sharing that `beforeEach` ran in 1.7-4.9 s, and forcing `--hookTimeout=1` reproduces the identical serialized `STACK_TRACE_ERROR` and stack frames. The hook does real work (mkdtemp + `git init` + 2× `git config` = three subprocess spawns), so under full-suite load it legitimately crosses 10 s — which also explains why the class hit a DIFFERENT file each time it was seen (index-rebuild / capture-turn / lazy-compress / MCP-server: all subprocess-heavy setups). Fix: `hookTimeout: 30_000` beside `testTimeout`, same rationale, one line. Gate re-run **5/5**. _(A raw-syscall probe of the same hook shape — 320 iterations, 8 concurrent workers — produced zero failures, which is what ruled out "flaky fs/git" and pointed at the timeout.)_ · Estimate: S · Depends: none · _**Trigger (D-248): next privacy-surface task OR the v0.6.3 cut, whichever first** — cheap enough to ride any privacy-adjacent lane; the gap is live on every capture today. Relates Task 233 (the finding), FR-15/design §6.6 (the screen contract), D-395._

shutil.rmtree('context/memory')"`, `node -e "require('fs').rmSync('context/memory',{recursive:true})"`, `os.remove`/`Path.unlink`/`ri` (PowerShell alias) all pass `touchesMemory` but fail `isDestructive`; (b) **the `Write`/`Edit` tool is not gated at all** — the guard wires only to `PreToolUse` for shell tools, so a `Write` with empty content truncates a memory file with no block (the async `observe-edit` fires AFTER); (c) **encoded/obfuscated commands** (`-EncodedCommand`, `iex`, `base64 -d | sh`) bypass BOTH the verb and path match since neither appears as a literal; (d) **`.claude/settings.json` isn't in `MEMORY_TOKENS`** — deleting the guard's own hook config disables it silently. **Design-first (this is a threat-surface expansion, not a one-line fix — get the over-block/under-block balance right per the guard's own "over-block is safe, under-block is data loss" philosophy):** ☐ extend `DESTRUCTIVE` with the language-level delete primitives (rmtree/rmSync/unlink/ri); ☐ add a `PreToolUse` gate for `Write|Edit|MultiEdit` whose resolved path is under a memory tier AND whose result is empty/near-empty vs the original; ☐ treat encoded-command markers as suspicious-by-default (block/confirm regardless of token match — an unreadable payload shouldn't get a free pass); ☐ add `.claude/settings.json`/`.local.json` + installed hook-script paths to `MEMORY_TOKENS`. **Done when:** each bypass class above has a test that now BLOCKS (or confirms), and the false-positive surface is measured (an ordinary `python -c` / `Write` to a non-memory path is NOT blocked). · Estimate: M–L (design the balance + implement + a real bypass-corpus test) · Depends: none · _**v0.5.x lane** (security depth; NOT a v0.5.1 blocker — the guard is DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH over the primary git-recoverability + audit-trail, and the bypasses need local shell access already; but real gaps against the stated model, so a near-term security slot). Relates D-192/D-193 (the guard's origin + the structural PreToolUse guardrail), `guard-memory.mjs`, the 2026-07-10 security review (findings 1/2/7/8), D-312._
