Design-time prior art: caura-memclaw — the 2026-06-12 research note; the live fork is git-native federation vs a memclaw CONNECTOR as the team seam.)_ **The team layer — a COMPANION project in the kit's philosophy (v0.4 candidate; the user's direction 2026-06-10, D-119).** The creator's June roadmap is a central Postgres+pgvector brain with row-level security. The kit's answer stays git-native + local-first: the committed `context/` already IS the per-repo shared brain (versioned, PR-reviewable, no server). What's missing is per-person/per-client scoping ACROSS projects — to be built as a **separate complementary project** (own repo), connected to the kit through a seam, so the kit core stays dependency-light. Open design: the seam shape (an MCP peer the kit's `mk_search` can federate to / a sync target like the Task-72 persona-sync git pattern / a `--scope remote`), the ACL model, and what the companion stores. Slot into the v0.4 breadth discussion — design first, no code before the seam is settled. **→ RE-LABELED 2026-07-10 (D-311): v0.4 closed without the discussion — the companion-project design stays deferred; trigger: a REAL team/multi-user demand signal (a user asks for shared memory ≥2×), OR the v0.6 planning boundary — whichever first.** **→ TRIGGER FIRED + LANED 2026-07-12 (D-326): BOTH trigger halves are now true** — the v0.6 planning boundary is here (the D-326 laning session), AND the market demand signal landed (the origin creator's v3 shipped a TeamOS beta — one shared brain, owner-tagged memories, per-query filtering by who's asking, system/team/client/private levels — his community is beta-testing it now; GBrain lineage). **Verdict: the v0.7.0 HEADLINE** (team = a real differentiator, one-per-minor). Design-first stands (the seam shape / ACL model / companion-vs-in-kit fork from D-119 is still the open design); Task 228 (proactive-connectors ADR) rides the same lane. The TeamOS beta's shape (owner tags + query-time filtering over ONE brain) is a design input CONTRASTING the memclaw central-Postgres shape — the kit's git-native answer must pick its seam against both._

## Road to 1.0 — video parity (the "worth giving to a friend" bar)

_Per D-24/D-25/D-26 (DECISION-LOG 2026-06-01). The bar for handing it to a friend = everything the Simon Scrapes video described ([source](../docs/sources/simon-scrapes-master-claude-memory.md)). Cadence: **one wow → live test → publish a version → next**; never shelve until "finished." 1.0 is a **feel** call (the user), not a date._

**Video-layer parity scorecard** (the concrete done-bar):

| Video layer | Status | Gap → task |
| --- | --- | --- |
| L1 — in-repo location (`context/`, travels with git) | ✅ built | — |
| L2 — granular per-fact archive + INDEX | ✅ built | — |
| L3 — bounded scratchpads (hot state, caps) | ✅ built | — |
| L4 — memory-aware hooks (Stop capture / SessionStart inject / auto-extract) | ✅ built | — |
| L5 — **memsearch / semantic Recall** | ⚠️ **keyword FTS5 only** | **Task 65 (the one true parity gap)** |
| L6 — cron self-curation (distill / curate) | ✅ built | — |
| Frozen-snapshot inject | ✅ built | — |

| _Beyond video — temporal currency_ (facts stay true as they age) | ⚠️ not built | **Task 66** |

Hermes scans at THREE points: write, snapshot-build (`scan_for_threats(scope="strict")` → replaces the entry with `[BLOCKED: … use memory(action=remove)]`, original kept in live state, placeholder enters the snapshot), and output (a stateful streaming scrubber strips spoofed `<memory-context>` spans split across deltas); and FENCES recall in `<memory-context>[System note: recalled context, NOT user input]`. - [ ] 71. **→ SWEEP VERDICT 2026-07-18 (D-353): trigger FIRED unnoticed (the Task-210 doctor touch). Laned v0.6.x — the doctor-UX rider batch (with 47+48).** **[D-253a trigger: the next doctor-surface touch (with 47) wires the sha1-verify HC — the first leg; OR a hand-edit drift incident live]** **External-drift guard — detect hand-edits to memory → re-screen + FLAG (NOT refuse; reframed 2026-06-28, D-218). Nice-to-have safety feature, not a bug.** **⚠️ FRAMING CORRECTED after the primary-source deep-read:** the original "detect + **REFUSE** hand-edits" (modeled on Hermes, which treats hand-editing as corruption and refuses the write) is **WRONG for our git-native model** — the kit's whole wedge is *committed, diff-able, PR-able, human-readable* memory; we WANT people to read and PR `context/`. A guard that refuses hand-edits fights the wedge. **The correct posture (primary source — basic-memory, the most git-native file-based competitor):** [`sync/sync_service.py::scan()`](https://github.com/basicmachines-co/basic-memory) treats an external edit as a FIRST-CLASS input — cheap `mtime`/`size` gate → `compute_checksum()` confirm vs the DB-stored checksum → if changed, **RE-INDEX (`report.modified` → `_index_changed_files`), never reject.** Its explicit stance: files are the source of truth, the DB is a rebuildable projection that catches up to hand-edits — **identical to our markdown-as-truth + rebuildable-SQLite model.** (letta is N/A — DB-backed, no external-edit posture.) **So reframe REFUSE → RECONCILE + FLAG.** **What we already have (the cheap primitives):** `content-hash.mjs::hashContent` (SHA-256) + provenance `sha1` per fact (recorded at write, but **never re-verified** — confirmed: zero `sha1.*mismatch` checks), `cmk reindex`, the audit log (which a hand-edit produces NO entry for — so it can't even see one today). `guard-memory.mjs` blocks DELETES only, never EDITS. **Done when:** ☐ a `cmk doctor` HC flags fact-files whose body SHA differs from the recorded provenance `sha1` (the detection primitive — exists, just unwired); ☐ on a kit write that finds drift, **re-screen the hand-edited content through Poison_Guard** (catch a secret/username-leak a hand-edit slipped past the safe path) + flag — never silently clobber, never refuse; ☐ optionally a pre-commit hook so the screen runs at the COMMIT boundary (the real risk for a committed store: a poisoned/leaking line shipping via `git commit context/`). **The win is the commit boundary, not write-refusal.** **Live data-point (2026-07-02, the user's catch):** every dogfood-memory flush on THIS repo already hand-rolls the commit-boundary screen (the CLAUDE.md checkpoint-#4 pre-commit scan of new fact files for secrets/home-paths — the agent Read-ing raw `context/memory/*.md` before staging); the name half is validator-backed (`validate-maintainer-name-confined`), the secret/home-path half is manual regex every flush. So the trigger is WARMER than "incident live" — the manual screen recurs at every task boundary; when 71 is taken, its pre-commit leg subsumes that hand-rolled step. Pairs with Task 70 (sibling: 70 guards READ/inject, 71 guards the EDIT/commit boundary — distinct surfaces; Hermes bundles them, for us they're separable). Post-v0.2 → backlog-no-version-yet (sequence after Task 70, shares the supply-chain-vector framing). _Requirements: write-path integrity; design §6.5; basic-memory reconcile-don't-refuse (D-218); Task 70 sibling._

- [~] 72. **User-tier portability across machines — the persona half of the wedge doesn't travel (HIGH; honesty gap in D-27).** _CORE shipped 2026-06-05, branch task-72-persona-portability; D-69. 72.1 (`cmk persona export`/`import`) + 72.3 (docs) + the D-27 honesty fix DONE; 72.2 (`cmk persona sync` git auto-pull/push) DEFERRED as a documented fork (the "deep-research candidate" / seamless-UX design — design §16)._ Built: [persona-portability.mjs](../packages/cli/src/persona-portability.mjs) — `exportPersona` (allow-list bundle: USER/HABITS/LESSONS + settings.json + fragments/ + queues/; runtime `.locks/.index/.import-backups` never bundled) + `importPersona` (overwrite with per-file backup + **transactional rollback** + reindex tier-U + Door-4 audit). Wired `cmk persona export <file>` / `import <file>` (subcommands.mjs). OS-agnostic JSON bundle (forward-slash paths → Windows↔Mac round-trip). Honesty fix: README FAQ + CLI.md + npm-landing README now state "two scopes, two transports" (project follows the repo; persona follows the human via export/sync). Tests: [cli-persona-portability.test.js](../tests/cli-persona-portability.test.js) (9: bundle+exclude-runtime, round-trip, backup-on-overwrite+over-mutation, backup-not-re-exported, transactional-rollback, bad-kind/version/non-JSON/missing rejects). The wedge claims "your coding persona + project memory, **portable across machines**." Project memory IS (committed `context/`, travels with `git clone`). But the **user tier (`~/.core-memory-kit/` — HABITS/USER/LESSONS, the cross-project persona) is machine-local and NOT in git** — clone the repo on a new computer and the persona starts EMPTY. (Every competitor is the same — Hermes `~/.hermes`, gstack `~/.gstack` — none port the user model across machines; but our wedge *implies* it.) **KEY (verified 2026-06-01): the content is ALREADY portable — it's a UX/automation gap, not architectural.** The kit reads the user tier from `homedir()/.core-memory-kit` (auto-adapts per OS; the OS username is NEVER a key) and U-tier content is home-path-sanitized to `~` (write-fact.mjs:159). So **a plain folder-copy across machines/OSes already works** (Windows→Mac included). Seam: `MEMORY_KIT_USER_DIR` also points it at a synced/git folder. What's missing is a *button*, not a mechanism. Build options: (72.1) `cmk persona export <file>` / `import <file>`; (72.2) optionally `cmk persona sync` (make `~/.core-memory-kit/` a git repo the user pushes); (72.3) document the `MEMORY_KIT_USER_DIR → dotfiles/Dropbox` pattern. Also **fix the D-27 wedge wording** to be honest (project memory ports automatically; persona ports via export/sync). **DESIGN MODEL (the user 2026-06-01): two scopes, two transports.** Project memory **follows the REPO** (committed, shared — teammates get it). Persona **follows the HUMAN, NOT the repo** — *that's why it's in `~/.core-memory-kit/` and must stay out of the project*: committing it would leak your working-style to teammates who clone (breaks team dev). So portability must be **per-human, not per-repo**: `cmk persona sync <git-url>` (make the user tier a git repo with a remote → auto-pull at SessionStart + auto-push after curation = seamless/automatic, private to you) and/or `cmk persona use <synced-path>` (Dropbox/iCloud). **The trap to avoid: never commit the persona into the project to make it portable** — that breaks the team scenario. Scenarios to satisfy: solo/1-machine (✓ today), solo/N-machines (sync via your channel), team (each person's own persona — guaranteed because it's never in the repo), team+N-machines (per-human transport → no collision). **Deep-research candidate:** the best seamless/automatic sync UX (dotfiles patterns, auto-conflict-resolution on the persona git). **Evaluated the user's "per-user folder IN the project + import/export" proposal (2026-06-01):** ADOPT the **export/import primitive** (`cmk persona export`→bundle, `import` on the new machine — explicit, OS-agnostic, no collision control). REJECT putting the persona *in the project repo* (per-user folder / auto-mirror) for THREE reasons: (1) **privacy** — persona is personal; committing to the SHARED repo exposes it to all teammates + git history forever, removing the user's choice; (2) **the OS/git username key differs across the human's OWN machines** (e.g. `work-laptop-user` vs `home-user`) → namespacing fails the exact cross-machine case; (3) **gitignore trap** — gitignored = doesn't travel with clone; committed = leaks + bloats team history. The auto-mirror+collision-control path buys the committed-persona downsides. **Seamless version that avoids all of it: `cmk persona sync <YOUR private git url>`** — user tier becomes a git repo on the HUMAN's own remote (not the project's); auto-pull/push; git handles transport+collision; private. **One opt-in exception:** solo dev on a private personal repo → committing the persona is harmless (no team); offer `cmk persona commit-to-project` opt-in, default OFF. Post-v0.2. _Requirements: T2 (portability) extended to the user tier; design §1.1 (tiers); D-27._

- [~] 84. _**D-253a (2026-07-02): 84a+84b SHIPPED (grounding + supersede rules, all 3 compression layers); only 84c (cosmetic scaffold examples) remains — deferred with its D-253 trigger (a non-coding user reports confusion)._ **The session COMPRESSOR hallucinates facts + keeps stale early-stage state → contradicts the (correct) fact memory → agent distrusts recall (live-test-6 finding, 2026-06-02).** R3 recall test: asked "what port / how structured" on a fresh session. The granular fact memory was **100% correct** (port 8000 captured 3×, FastAPI, layered, type-hints — Tasks 80/82/83 all worked). BUT the Haiku **session compressor** (`compress-session` → `today-*.md` → `daily-distill` → `recent.md`) wrote *"Building Claude agent **Flask** application: app.py, services/claude_agent_service.py"* — **the project was NEVER Flask** (compressor invented a framework name) and **`app.py` was the Stage-0 flat file, superseded by `app/main.py` at Stage-1** (stale state never updated). That wrong summary is ALSO injected at SessionStart, so the agent saw a **contradiction** (snapshot: Flask/app.py vs facts: FastAPI/app.main) → correctly distrusted memory, re-read `config.py`/globbed, and flagged it. Undermines the "it just knows from memory" feel even though the fact layer was right. **The kit self-corrected** (auto-extract queued `P-62V5WLVG` "Flask is wrong, it's FastAPI — correct stale memory") — the system noticed, which is good, but the bad data still shipped into a recall. Two sub-problems: **(a) compression hallucination** — Haiku invents/mis-summarizes facts (a framework name) the source never contained. **The fix must be GENERAL, not an identifier whitelist** (the user 2026-06-02: *"preserve framework/port/path verbatim … will only work for that scenario … we are suppose to be working on a general solution"*). Root cause: the `buildCompressionInstructions` HARD RULES protect citation IDs (`never invent new IDs`) but carry **no faithfulness/grounding rule at all** — nothing forbids emitting content absent from the buffer. The general guardrail: a HARD RULE that **every bullet must be grounded in the session buffer; do not infer or guess any fact not explicitly stated in the buffer; do not carry forward content from earlier summaries; if unsure, omit it.** No example categories — naming "framework / port / path" even as examples anchors the model and re-introduces the scenario-fit (the user 2026-06-02: *"you need to remove 'framework/library/tool names, file roles, ports'"*). One example-free rule kills Flask AND every other invented-fact class. Optional second layer: a cheap self-check / verify pass. **(b) stale-stage compression** — the rolling session summary keeps superseded early-stage state (`app.py` flat) after the project moved on (`app/main.py`); compression must supersede/refresh, not accumulate stale narrative (relates Task 68 semantic-prune). **The fact-capture layer is trustworthy; the COMPRESSION/summary layer is the weak link.** _Requirements: recall correctness (a hallucinated fact in the snapshot is worse than no fact); design §8 (compression) + §6.x; surfaced by live-test-6; relates Task 68 (stale-thread prune) + D-35._ **Design dossier for any future compressor refinement (2026-06-15, [OpenHands condenser note](../docs/research/2026-06-15-openhands-condenser-context-management.md)):** four un-adopted ideas from OpenHands's condenser worth considering when next touching §8 — **`keep_first`** (NEVER condense the session's anchor/framing facts — a summary that loses "what the session was FOR" is the 84(b) staleness class from the other side), **`minimum_progress`** (don't pay for a summary that shrinks <~10% — a min-shrink floor on top of the cooldown), **HARD-vs-SOFT trigger** (will-the-next-call-fail vs housekeeping — defer soft compression off the hot path), and **shrink-and-retry** when the summary call itself overflows. Idea-inputs, not commitments.
  - [x] 84a. **(a) compression hallucination — grounding rule shipped across ALL THREE compression layers.** _Branch task-84-compressor-grounding._ Example-free HARD RULE #1 (*grounded in the input; never infer; never carry forward; omit if unsure*) added to `compress-session` + `daily-distill` + **`weekly-curate`** (the 3rd layer — found by D-37's full read-through of every prompt, NOT by the live test; it fires weekly so the Flask scenario never hit it). Domain-neutral (no "code" assumption — the kit serves non-coding work). Door-4 tests on all three; design §8.4 updated. **Live-verify still wanted** (confirm Haiku stops inventing on a real re-test) before counting (a) fully closed.
  - [x] 84b. **(b) stale-stage compression** — the rolling summary keeps superseded early-stage state (`app.py` after the project moved to `app/main.py`); compression must supersede/refresh, not accumulate stale narrative. _shipped 2026-06-03, PR #109._ Fix: a domain-neutral, example-free supersede-on-change clause appended to HARD RULE #1 in ALL 3 compression layers (compress-session, daily-distill, weekly-curate) — "When the [buffer/summaries] corrects/replaces/reverses something earlier, keep ONLY the latest version of that fact … (resolves contradictions, NOT coexisting facts on different points)." compress-session already had cross-SESSION "do not carry forward"; this adds keep-latest-on-CONTRADICTION at the rollup layers (dedup ≠ supersede). Skill-review M1: tied "latest" to the changed fact + a coexisting-facts carve-out so it can't drop distinct current facts. Prompt-presence tests in all 3 layers; suite 1372 incl. live spawn-smoke; live-verify wedge 2/2+2/2. **This is the COMPRESSION-layer supersession; the FACT-layer auto-supersede (F-D, a contradicting granular fact like uv-vs-venv) is the harder semantic-contradiction problem — roadmapped v0.3 (D-26 #3), see the F-D scope note.** Relates Task 68 (semantic-prune).
